each was invisible to the previous pass: one was masked by `try?`, the other only reachable
from a hostile peer.

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC74 | **`pq.ctr` reached a trapping `UInt32(_:)` conversion — persistent remote crash (fixed).** `PQRCSession.decrypt` already range-checked `header.n`/`header.pn` before building the AD, with a comment naming the exact attack ("a crafted header (`n: -1`) … would crash the app — and re-crash on relaunch if the relay replays it"). The PQ-rekey counter `pq.ctr` — an unbounded `Int` straight off the wire — was missed. It flows to `DoubleRatchet.refreshChain`, which does `Data(uint32BE: UInt32(counter))`; `UInt32(Int)` **traps** (it does not truncate) on a negative or >2^32 value. The rekey is applied at step 5 of `DoubleRatchet.decrypt`, **before** the AEAD open, so an established contact (who legitimately holds our current KEM pubkey and can therefore compute a matching `tgt` and a well-formed 1088-byte `ct`) could abort the process with an unauthenticated header — and re-abort on every relaunch as the relay replays the event. Violates invariant 12 / SPEC §12 ("garbage in known fields is never fatal"). **Fix (two layers):** (1) `PQRCSession.decrypt` extends the existing guard to `header.pq?.ctr`; (2) `refreshChain` uses `UInt32(truncatingIfNeeded:)` so the conversion cannot trap for any future caller — byte-identical in range, so the frozen vectors are unchanged (verified: PQRCCore 82 green, PQRCNostr chaos matrix 99 green). Pinned by `SessionTests.decrypt_outOfRangeHeaderCounter_throwsNotTraps`. Honest scope: the trap is proven-by-reading, not proven-by-exploit — the regression test pins the guard (rejects the header as malformed), it does not craft a matching `tgt`. | app-only |
| AC75 | **Huginn silently lost the LLM token on every relaunch — the file-keychain mirror deleted the data-protection item (fixed).** `ConfigurationStore.saveTokenToKeychain()` writes the token twice: to the data-protection keychain (Huginn's own prompt-free reads) and to the legacy FILE keychain (the launcher's `/usr/bin/security` read, which cannot see DP items). `KeychainBox.save()` opens with a `SecItemDelete`, and the file-keychain box built its query with an **empty** keychain selector (`useDataProtection: false` → `[:]`). **Omitting `kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain` does not mean "file keychain" — the query matches items in EITHER keychain.** So the mirror's delete matched the DP item (same service + account) and wiped the token that had just been written. Net effect: `save()` appeared to succeed (`try?` swallowed nothing — the save genuinely worked, then was undone), `init` read the DP keychain back as `nil`, fell through to the env-file token that C-8 had deliberately removed, and the token came back **empty** — so the Mac AI could not authenticate to its LLM after any restart. This is the bug behind the "re-enter the LLM token in Huginn's settings" note in meatsuittasks, which had been attributed to the bundle-id rename. **Fix:** set the selector explicitly in both directions (`[kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain: useDataProtection]`), never `[:]`, keeping the two keychains disjoint. Proven: the two red `LLMTokenAtRestTests` now pass and the full Huginn suite is green. Pinned by the new `KeychainIsolationTests` (the mirror must not clobber the DP item, and deleting one must not delete the other). | app-only |
| AC76 | **`BackendRegistryTests.perAIKeyAccount_matchesLegacyRule` was stale-red (fixed).** The test still expected `acp` in the per-AI-API-key set, but the registry had correctly moved `acp` ("Mac-Tethered-AI") to `usesPerAIKey: false` — it needs no API key in this app, because its LLM credential lives on the Mac node (Huginn settings / `ELDR_LLM_TOKEN`). The **test** was wrong, not the code; expectation corrected and the rule's rationale written into `ConfiguredAI.apiKeyAccount`. Also: `Eldrctl` had 9 passing tests but was **missing from the CI package matrix** — added. | app-only |

## Second audit wave — fan-out review (crypto / wire / node / app / docs), 2026-07-11 — `[app-only]`

A six-surface parallel audit (with adversarial verification) on top of the AC74–76 pass.
Every FIXED item below was verified green before commit. The DEFERRED items are real but
lower-severity (proximity/trusted-peer attackers, or latent/not-yet-reachable); each has its
fix recorded so it is not re-discovered from scratch.

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC77 | **Shell env leaked the agent's secrets — FIXED.** `run_shell`/`open_terminal` spawned their child with `ToolExecutor.shellEnvironment` = the agent's full `ProcessInfo` environment, which carries `ELDR_LLM_TOKEN`, `ELDR_ACP_METADATA_KEY`, `SYBILCLAW_GATEWAY_TOKEN`. A prompt-injected model with one approved `run_shell` could `env` them into its own context (self-exfiltration, no network). Fix: strip the exact secret keys + any secret-shaped key in a namespace we own (`ELDR_`/`PQRC_`/`SYBILCLAW_`); reads spawn no shell so no capability lost. Test `runShell_doesNotLeakAgentSecretsToTheShell`. Node-audit F1 (HIGH). | app-only |
| AC78 | **`eldrctl` ssh/scp argument injection — FIXED.** `--target` reached `ssh`/`scp` as bare argv with only a non-empty check; a leading `-` (e.g. `-oProxyCommand=…`) is parsed by getopt as a client OPTION → arbitrary local program. Reachable because `eldrctl` is documented as AI-drivable. Fix: `ConduitProvisioner.isSafeSSHDestination` (unit-tested) rejects a leading `-` + whitelists `[user@]host[:port]`; `scp` gets a `--` separator. Node-audit F2 (MED). | app-only |
| AC79 | **`processedWrapIDs` unbounded — FIXED.** The envelope-dedupe set was never evicted. The wrap layer is ECDH-encrypted to the recipient's PUBLIC key, so ANY network party knowing the victim's npub can mint distinct wraps that `unwrap` structurally accepts → unbounded growth → OOM. Fix: oldest-out FIFO, cap 8192; the ratchet's one-time keys remain the real replay backstop. Test `processedWrapIDs_isBounded`. Wire-audit F1 (HIGH). | app-only |
| AC80 | **Chunk-reassembly bound (relay ACP/MCP) — FIXED** (see also the AC74-pass reassembly work): `RelayFraming` now caps declared `total` (`maxChunksPerLine`) and the count of concurrently-incomplete lines (`maxPendingReassemblies`, LRU). Wire-audit F3 / node corroboration. | app-only |
| AC81 | **ACP client permission default was allow-all — FIXED.** `ACPClientHandler`/`ACPClient`/`ACPClientDriver` defaulted `requestPermission` to `{ _,_ in true }`. Not live (both production paths override: read-only bridge → `false`, phone path → `decidePermission`), but a fail-OPEN default violates SPEC §0. Flipped to deny; zero blast radius (all suites green). Node-audit F3 + wire/app corroboration. | app-only |
| AC82 | **Egress-firewall account default was device-global — FIXED (CRITICAL).** The master name-redaction control's per-account default used a bare `egressFirewallEnabled` UserDefaults key, so one silo's toggle changed every deniable silo's redaction. Now per-silo (`egressFirewallEnabled(siloID:)`), default account migrates the legacy value once, others default ON. App-audit F3. | app-only |
| AC83 | **Window-reply cross-conversation TOCTOU — FIXED (CRITICAL).** `RuntimeSink.postAgentReply` resolved its destination from the live global `windowConversation()` at POST time, after the cross-actor provider await — so a human switching the (single, global) window to chat B mid-turn made chat A's context post into B, agent-signed. Fix: thread the gate-time `conversationID` through `runWindowReply`→`postAgentReply` and re-verify the window still holds it (drop otherwise, fail closed). Test asserts the engine pins the destination. App-audit F1. | app-only |
| AC84 | **Stop on a stale window killed a live one — FIXED (HIGH).** `endMyAIWindow` cleared the single global engine window unconditionally, so "stop" in a conversation that didn't hold the window tore down a different chat's live window with no closing announcement. Now guarded on `myWindowConversationID == conversationID`. App-audit F2. | app-only |
| AC85 | **Coding "ask each time" showed a stale consent state — FIXED (MED).** Disabling "run commands" reset the local `codingAutonomy` @State but not the persisted `autonomousChangesConsent` that `decidePermission` reads, so the UI said "ask each time" while auto-approve was still on. Now persists the reset. App-audit F5. Not a fail-open (enforcement read the persisted value), a trust/honesty bug. | app-only |
| AC86 | **DEFERRED — free-text AI instructions + custom skills in cleartext UserDefaults (MED).** `ConfiguredAI.instructions` and `CustomSkill.instruction/summary/name` (user-authored prose) persist via plain `UserDefaults`, bypassing the app's `EncryptedStore` boundary that every other content record uses — visible in an unencrypted backup / forensic image. Not remotely triggerable. Fix (planned): route these free-text fields through `EncryptedStore` seal/open or a per-silo Keychain blob. App-audit F4. | tech-debt |
| AC87 | **DEFERRED — unbounded discovery/subscription caches (LOW).** `nearbyBundles` (proximity attacker), `NostrWebSocketTransport`'s inbound `AsyncThrowingStream` (default `.unbounded` — a flooding relay), and `NearbyRelayHub.subTasks` (an already-authed companion) lack the bounded-eviction the same files already apply to `pendingRequests`/`pendingRetry`. Attacker models are narrow (co-present, or a trusted/authed peer). Fix (planned): apply the same capped-eviction pattern; for the stream pass an explicit `.bufferingNewest(N)`. Wire-audit F2/F4/F5. | tech-debt |
| AC88 | **DEFERRED — generated `~/.config/eldr-acp/env` heredoc quoting (LOW).** `ConduitProvisioner` writes the env file via an unquoted heredoc; an embedded `'` in a `--llm-url`/`--llm-model`/`--relay` value breaks the single-quote literal. NOT currently reachable (grep confirms nothing sources that file — the LaunchAgent execs a literal argv), so LOW. Fix (planned): `printf '%s\n'` per line, matching the adjacent OWNER/WORKDIR lines. Node-audit F4. | tech-debt |

## tech-week — cloud-CLI harness integration (WS1–WS3) + final verification pass, 2026-07-15 — `[app-only]` / `[tech-debt]`

The `tech-week` branch: command chips in the interactive terminal (WS1), the silent-bypass
indicator (WS2), and the cloud-CLI harness work (WS3a–f: env scrub, vendor-key Keychain seam,
`runHarness` in production hosts, on-device descriptor verification, `delegate_to_cloud_agent`
brokering, distinct consent + live indicator). A Fable-5 verification pass reviewed the full
branch diff, fixed what it found (AC91), and recorded the honest deferrals (AC92–AC94).
Every FIXED item below was verified green before commit (all 7 packages, full EldrChat +
Huginn suites).

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC89 | **WS2 — the node self-reports its ungated state via a non-standard `initialize` field.** The real defect behind "prompts aren't working" was that `ELDR_ACP_ALLOW_UNGATED_TOOLS` (or the phone's own standing consent) skipped the permission card SILENTLY. The agent now advertises `eldrAllowUngatedTools` in `agentCapabilities` (eldr-prefixed so a spec-compliant ACP client ignores it); the phone shows a persistent orange banner when either cause is active, tap-to-revoke for its own consent. Honest limits: the banner's autonomy half reads a local `@State` mirror refreshed on appear (a Details-sheet toggle doesn't repaint it until the view reappears), and `acpNodeUngatedByConversation` is only updated by the next `initialize`, not cleared on unpair. Pinned by `initialize_advertisesUngatedToolsWhenConfigured`, `ungatedNode_advertisesItInStartResult`, `eventObserverReceivesUngatedToolsAdvertisement`. | app-only |
| AC90 | **WS3e/f — delegation is double-gated and its consent is DISTINCT, keyed off node-authored title prefixes.** `delegate_to_cloud_agent` requires (1) the node operator's `cloudAgentDelegationEnabled` (env `ELDR_ACP_ALLOW_CLOUD_DELEGATION`, default off, checked before any spawn) AND (2) a phone permission card routed — by the `"Delegate to cloud agent"` / `"Delegated action"` title prefixes (`ACPCloudDelegation`) — to a NEW `cloudAgentDelegationConsent`, never the general `autonomousChangesConsent`; "Allow always" on a delegation card flips only the delegation consent. Trust model, stated honestly: title-keyed routing follows the `open_terminal` precedent — it defends against a confused/prompt-injected MODEL on an honest node; a malicious node binary can lie in titles (it can lie about everything) and is out of scope phone-side. The purple "cloud agent running" indicator brackets the OUTER tool_call's pending→completed lifecycle, so it also shows while the approval card is up (delegation *requested*, not yet running) — accepted as honest-enough. Pinned by the three `delegation_*` e2e tests (real relay-ACP path) + the fail-closed `delegateToCloudAgent_*` agent tests. | app-only |
| AC91 | **Verification-pass fixes (Fable 5, full-diff review).** (a) `delegate_to_cloud_agent` was ADVERTISED to the model even when the operator gate was off — a tool that always refuses; now filtered from the advertised set when disabled, the `runOneTool` guard kept as defense-in-depth (`delegateTool_advertisedOnlyWhenDelegationEnabled`). (b) `session/cancel` did not stop a live delegation — the spawned CLI ran to completion and the turn couldn't end until it did, violating the same fail-closed rule `terminateTerminals` enforces for PTYs; the delegated prompt is now raced against the cancel poll (mirroring `runModelCall`) and the transport is closed on cancel, killing the child. (c) `eldr-node --responder eldr-acp` printed the cloud-harness status line ("vendor key MISSING…") because `HarnessRegistry.descriptor(id:)` matches the built-in too; only a `.stdioSpawn` hit is now treated as a cloud harness. (d) removed the dead `AppModel.silentBypassActive` (ConversationView owns the live computation). | app-only |
| AC92 | **DEFERRED — the delegation path injects NO vendor key.** `delegateToCloudAgent` spawns from the raw `HarnessRegistry` descriptor; `withVendorKey` is only wired on the TOP-LEVEL responder paths (Huginn relay host, `eldr-node --responder`). The delegated CLI authenticates only via its own login cache (`claude /login`, gemini OAuth — the common case) or an operator-exported env var, which the WS3b design explicitly wanted to keep out of the general node env. Planned fix: a vendor-key-resolver seam on `AgentConfig` (an equality-exempt closure, like `logRedactor`), wired per-host to its own Keychain. Cancel-kill behavior is compile/logic-verified only — an e2e test needs an injectable registry (the lookup is hardcoded), same seam. | tech-debt |
| AC93 | **DEFERRED — the nested permission proxy is not e2e-tested against a real permission-issuing harness.** A delegated CLI's own file/shell actions are forwarded through the outer `requestPermission` with the `"Delegated action"` prefix (each a fresh `delegate-<uuid>` toolCallId never announced via `tool_call` — fine for our phone client, which keys cards on title/kind, but unproven against other ACP clients). Exercising it needs a real credentialed CLI or a scripted ACP-speaking stub + the AC92 registry seam. `session/prompt` against real vendor credentials also remains untested (WS3d verified launch + `initialize` only). | tech-debt |
| AC94 | **PATH is the real cloud-CLI deployment blocker, not code.** nvm-installed `claude-code-acp`/`gemini` live in `~/.nvm/versions/node/<v>/bin`, which a GUI-launched Huginn.app / launchd `eldr-node` does not have (verified: spawn with the system default PATH fails with "No such file or directory"). Bare-name descriptors resolve only for a PATH-visible install (Homebrew/system Node) or an extended node PATH — a per-Mac setup step, documented in the registry comment + Bridge UI copy. Also: the `@zed-industries/claude-code-acp` npm package is deprecated in favor of `@agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp`, whose binary is `claude-agent-acp` — the descriptor's `command` must change if the successor is installed instead. | app-only |

## tech-week — A2A (Agent2Agent) v1.0 integration + SwiftA2A SDK, 2026-07-16 — `[app-only]` / `[tech-debt]`

Google's A2A protocol (Linux Foundation, spec v1.0) adopted for agent↔agent task delegation:
a new clean-room `Packages/SwiftA2A` SDK (zero non-Apple deps, built for later extraction and
proposal to a2aproject as the official Swift SDK), an `.a2aRemote` harness kind bridging
`delegate_to_cloud_agent` to remote A2A agents (`A2AACPBridge`), a loopback A2A server on the
node, and an E2EE A2A-over-PQRC transport profile (`docs/A2A-PQRC-EXTENSION.md`). ACP remains
the phone↔agent protocol — A2A is complementary (agent↔agent), not a replacement. Wire format
pinned fixture-first against `a2a.proto` + the spec's canonical JSON (16 frozen fixtures).

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC95 | **Push notifications (webhooks) permanently unsupported.** A2A's push mechanism has the client register a webhook URL with a remote agent — publishing the client's address to an arbitrary server. `capabilities.pushNotifications` is always `false`; all four `*TaskPushNotificationConfig` methods return `PushNotificationNotSupportedError` (-32003). SSE streaming + `GetTask` polling cover the async case; over PQRC the tunnel is bidirectional anyway. Privacy-maximizing per SPEC §0; spec-legal (capability is optional). | app-only |
| AC96 | **The served agent card is auth-gated — a deliberate spec deviation.** The spec intends `/.well-known/agent-card.json` as public discovery; a card enumerating this Mac's agent skills is a metadata leak, so the node's `A2AHTTPServer` requires the bearer token on EVERY route including the card, binds `127.0.0.1` only (`NWParameters.requiredLocalEndpoint` pins loopback at the socket layer), serves no TLS (traffic never leaves the device; remote access is the PQRC binding, where the ratchet replaces HTTPS), and is OFF by default behind an explicit Bridge toggle + per-task approval (deny/timeout → `REJECTED`, fail closed). | app-only |
| AC97 | **Text-only bounds on A2A Parts.** `Part.url` is NEVER fetched (rendered as a literal link — no blob path, no SSRF); `Part.raw` beyond 32 KB is skipped with an explicit notice; `Part.data` is rendered as compact JSON text. Enforced in `A2AACPBridge`, not policy. Also: `raw` stays base64-string on the wire type so a relayed payload round-trips even when it isn't valid base64 (the spec's own example isn't). | app-only |
| AC98 | **A2A 0.3 compatibility rejected.** Per spec, an absent/empty `A2A-Version` header means 0.3; this implementation is 1.0-only, so absent/0.3 → `VersionNotSupportedError` (-32009). v1.0 renamed the JSON-RPC methods to the proto RPC names (`SendMessage`, `GetTask`, …) — the 0.3 `message/send` style is a different wire dialect we never speak. | app-only |
| AC99 | **AgentCard JWS signatures parsed, not verified.** `signatures` round-trip losslessly and verification sits behind the `AgentCardSignatureVerifier` seam, but the default is a no-op returning `.unverified` (JWS + RFC 8785 canonicalization is real machinery). Required before proposing the SDK upstream. Until then, card trust = the transport's (HTTPS / bearer / kind-10420 peer). | tech-debt |
| AC100 | **`INPUT_REQUIRED`/`AUTH_REQUIRED` fail the delegation.** Delegation is one-shot task semantics and permission cards are allow/deny, not free-text; an interrupted A2A task surfaces the agent's question/credential guidance in the tool result ("re-delegate with more context") and ends the turn. Multi-turn task continuation is a v2 item. | tech-debt |
| AC101 | **SecurityRequirement wire shape follows the spec DOC, not the proto.** The canonical card example serializes `security` OpenAPI-style (`{"scheme": ["scopes"]}`) where strict proto3-JSON of `security_requirements` would nest `{"schemes":{…:{"list":[…]}}}`. SwiftA2A decodes BOTH, encodes the documented shape (what deployed agents publish). Flagged for an upstream spec-consistency issue when the SDK is proposed. | app-only |

## tech-week — bug-sweep pass (WS0/A1), 2026-07-16 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC102 | **A1 — mutating tools in the My-AI chat bridge are permission-gated via the phone, superseding CR-1's read-only stance.** The watch-along/My-AI chat runner (`ACPDriverAgentRunner`) no longer hard-denies mutating tools (`requestPermission {_,_ in false}` + `ELDR_ACP_TOOLS=read_file,list_dir`): each `write_file`/`edit_file`/`run_shell`/`open_terminal`/`delegate_to_cloud_agent` request is routed to the OWNER'S PHONE over a new tiny E2EE control channel (`ACPPermissionChannel`, `ACPPERM1\|<b64url json>` frames on the existing ratcheted mesh — no new crypto, SPEC §2) into the SAME `PersonaRuntime.decidePermission` ladder as the phone-driven coding path (standing consent → allow; else approval card; no asker → deny). Fail-closed at every seam: node denies on timeout (120 s, mirroring C-1) / teardown / malformed frame; phone services requests only from a paired `coding_agent` contact and always swallows recognized frames (never rendered as chat); the runner FORCES `ELDR_ACP_ALLOW_UNGATED_TOOLS=0` so the operator's trusted-local escape hatch can never make this prompt-injectable path run unattended (CR-1's actual threat stays blocked). Tool-advertising honesty survives as the degenerate case: a client with no permission route declares `eldrCanPrompt:false` at `session/new` and the agent then filters mutating tools from the advertised set + adds a system line saying where they ARE available. WS0 rides along: `authenticate` is now a spec-compliant success no-op and `session/set_mode`/`session/set_model` answer benignly (Xcode 27's newer ACP seed treated our `-32601` as "requires authentication"). | app-only |

## tech-week — WS-MLX: full MLX manager GUI in Huginn, 2026-07-17 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC103 | **WS-MLX — a dedicated "MLX" tab in Huginn wraps the whole `mlx_lm` CLI surface** (`MLXService` + `MLXSupport` + `MLXView`, pattern: `ContextGraphService`): env install, OpenAI-compatible server with one-click "Use as Eldr LLM backend" (writes `llmURL`/`llmModel` through `ConfigurationStore` — the standard `LLMClient` seam, no new abstraction), HF model manager, convert/quantize, one-shot generate playground, LoRA fine-tune + fuse. Judgment calls, each verified against a real `mlx-lm 0.31.3` install: **(a)** the toolchain lives in a private venv at `~/.config/eldr-acp/mlx/venv` (uv preferred — fetches a managed CPython 3.12; `python3 -m venv` fallback), so nothing touches the system Python and every subcommand runs as `venv-python -m mlx_lm.<sub>` (survives the `huggingface-cli`→`hf` console-script churn). **(b)** Model list/delete reads the HF hub cache layout natively (`HFCache`) instead of parsing `mlx_lm.manage --scan` tables — same directories, deterministic, and `--delete`'s interactive y/N prompt can't hang a GUI subprocess; delete moves the `models--…` folder to the Trash (permanent removal only if the Trash is unavailable). Downloads warm the cache via `snapshot_download` with the repo id validated by a strict charset before embedding (injection-safe). **(c)** Fine-tune drives `mlx_lm.lora --config <generated yaml> --train`: the LoRA rank/scale/dropout exist only as config keys (no CLI flags), and the config keys survived the `--lora-layers`→`--num-layers` flag rename. **(d)** Server sampling defaults (`--max-tokens/--temp/--top-p`) are emitted only when set, so an older mlx-lm without them still starts; `--prompt=`/`--chat-template=` use the `=`-form so values starting with `-` can't be eaten by argparse. **(e)** launchd autostart = plist (`chat.eldr.mlx-server`, RunAtLoad, KeepAlive **false** — a bad model must not crash-loop at login) + `bootstrap`/`bootout`; exactly one owner of the port at a time (enabling waits for the in-app child to exit before bootstrapping; child-mode servers are SIGTERMed on app quit so they can't orphan-squat the port). **(f)** Jobs are exclusive (one at a time — two model loads can OOM a Mac), stream merged stdout/stderr into a `\r`-aware terminal buffer (tqdm bars render as one live line), and cancel via SIGTERM→SIGKILL with a bounded reap. **(g)** Playground is one-shot `mlx_lm.generate` (the `mlx_lm.chat` REPL is not PTY-driven; Test Chat covers conversational use once the server is wired as backend). Note: a playground prompt travels in the child's argv (visible to local `ps` while running) — same exposure class as `run_shell`; it never leaves the machine and no chat/message payload flows through the MLX tab at all. OSLog for MLX events carries kinds/ports/exit codes only; model ids and paths stay in the in-memory DiagnosticsLog (invariant 12). | app-only |

## tech-week — A2/A3/A4: context-budget upgrades, AGENTS.md discovery, version surface, 2026-07-17 — `[app-only]` / `[tech-debt]`

Three additive agent-plumbing items on top of the existing per-result head+tail truncation
and whole-message trimming (they add stages, they don't replace them). All knobs thread
through the existing `AgentConfig` env plumbing and Huginn's `ConfigurationStore` →
`ConfigurationView` Context-budget section. Pinned by new Swift Testing suites in
`ContextBudgetTests`, `ProjectMemoryTests`, `ACPAgentTests`/`UnitTests`, and Huginn's
`ConfigStoreTests`.

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC104 | **A2 spill-to-file location = `<workdir>/.eldr/tool-results/`, INSIDE the path jail.** When a tool result exceeds `maxToolResultBytes` the whole result is written to a fresh file under the session workdir's `.eldr/tool-results/` and the model is handed head+tail PLUS that relative path, so it can `read_file` the elided middle instead of losing it. The path is run through `ToolExecutor.jailedPath` so it can only ever land inside the jail (a spilled file `read_file` can reach, nothing escaping the workdir). The spill note's bytes are RESERVED out of `maxToolResultBytes`, so the model-facing text still fits the budget — spilling never re-inflates the window. The file is written in cleartext (it MIRRORS the unredacted tool result the model already sees — redacting it would defeat the point) and is NOT a log/metadata sink, so the C-6 log redactor and the events.jsonl canary do not apply; it sits beside the project files the agent already reads/writes. Default ON; off via `ELDR_ACP_TOOL_RESULT_SPILL=0`. A bare `ToolExecutor` (the agent-layer-free init) defaults spill OFF so it never touches disk. **No automatic GC of spilled files** — they accumulate under `.eldr/` until the user (or a `.gitignore` + clean) removes them. | tech-debt |
| AC105 | **A2 tool-result aging keeps the last N=4 verbatim, stubs older ones, and runs BEFORE any whole-message drop.** `ContextBudget.trim` gained a `keepRecentToolResults` stage (env `ELDR_ACP_TOOL_RESULT_KEEP`, default 4) that replaces the BODY of every `tool` message except the most-recent N with a one-line `[tool result aged out: …]` stub — in place, preserving order/role/`toolCallId` so tool_call↔tool pairing stays valid — before the existing turn-count cap and char-budget elision. It is UNCONDITIONAL (the industry compaction pattern: old bulky results are stubbed even when the budget isn't yet blown), which is a behavior change from the prior "trim only when over budget" but keeps message COUNT identical (elides content, never drops). `stubPrefix`/`elisionPrefix` are distinct so the two stages never re-stub each other. The TRAILING run of `tool` messages — the current batch, which `trim` sees BEFORE the model call that first consumes it — is never stubbed regardless of N (adversarial-pass fix: the first cut counted the unread batch toward the global keep-window, so a batch larger than N lost its earliest results before the model ever read them, and keep=0 stubbed every result before its first read). `keepRecentToolResults=0` therefore stubs all but the current batch; a large value effectively disables aging. | app-only |
| AC106 | **A3 context precedence is `ELDR_ACP_CONTEXT_FILE` > per-project `eldr.md` > `<cwd>/AGENTS.md`; A4 version is a compile-time constant, not build-stamped.** `ProjectContext.read` now falls back to a repo-root `AGENTS.md` (the industry-standard file claude-code/gemini-cli/Xcode also read) after the explicit override and the auto-discovered `eldr.md`; loaded once per session and prepended to the system prompt, unchanged mechanics. A concise repo-root `AGENTS.md` was authored for Eldr itself. For A4, SPM has no clean build-time date/commit injection, so `ACPAgent.agentBuild` is a hand-bumped compile-time constant (the plan explicitly allowed this); it surfaces in `--version` (`eldr-acp/<semver> (build <date>)`), the ACP `initialize` response (`agentInfo.build`, alongside the unchanged `version`), and Huginn (`ConfigurationStore.expectedAgentVersion` = the compiled-in baseline; `installedAgentVersion()` runs the installed binary's `--version`). The staleness WARNING + reinstall button (WS-B3) is out of scope — this only makes the version READABLE; a constant that a developer forgets to bump would under-report staleness. | tech-debt |
| AC107 | **Docs consolidated into `docs/` (2026-07-17).** The canonical specs moved from the repo root into `docs/` as real files (`SPEC.md` → `docs/pqrc-SPEC-v1_1.md`; `APP-SPEC.md`; `TEST-PLAN.md` — the three symlinks are gone), the root strays moved in (`TESTFLIGHT-GUIDE.md`, `meatsuittasks.md`, `marketing/lerants-agentic-writeup-notes.md`; the `marketing/` folder is removed), and superseded/one-shot artifacts now live under `docs/deprecated/` (formerly `docs/old/`, plus `AI-WINDOW-PER-CONVERSATION.md`, `PROMPT-AUDIT.md`, `UX-RECOMMENDATIONS.md`, both dated huginn-gateway notes, and `TestVectors/THREAT_MODEL-old.md`) — nothing was deleted. Deliberately KEPT live because code/docs reference them as sources of truth: `eldrchat-agent-skills.md` (APP-SPEC §23 / `AgentSkills.swift`) and `ENTERPRISE-PITCH.md` (AC46 in-app tour source). CLAUDE.md + AGENTS.md updated (layout note, eight-package test loop now incl. `SwiftA2A`). Root keeps only `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` (tooling-convention files). **[Correction, 2026-07-24: `docs/deprecated/` was renamed `docs/done/2026-07-17/` when the documentation lifecycle was formalised — retired docs are now filed by retirement date. Paths in this entry are historical. See `docs/README.md` and `docs/done/README.md`.]** | app-only |

## tech-week — trusted-mode tools + tether persistence, 2026-07-17 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC108 | **The persistent "Run tools without asking permission" toggle is now honored on EVERY Huginn-hosted path, superseding AC102's "runner forces `ELDR_ACP_ALLOW_UNGATED_TOOLS=0`" clause.** Operator directive: per-call approval was killing tool-using models (approval card rendered under sheets on the phone, auto-deny/hang on timeout), and an owner who explicitly accepted the risk kept being overridden. Changes: (a) `ACPDriverAgentRunner` passes the operator's live toggle instead of forcing 0 — the force was ALSO provably ineffective whenever the launcher was used, because the launcher re-`source`s the env file after Huginn's overrides, so the file's value silently won; the code now says what actually happens. (b) `startRelayACPHost` builds its `AgentConfig` from the shared env file instead of `AgentConfig.default`, which had silently discarded the toggle plus every context-budget/tool-allowlist/timeout setting for phone remote-drive. (c) The toggle flips ON only through an are-you-sure `confirmationDialog` spelling out unattended execution + prompt-injection risk; it does not expire, and OFF applies instantly with no dialog. Fail-closed is unchanged when OFF; the phone keeps the `eldrAllowUngatedTools` bypass indicator (WS2) either way. CR-1's prompt-injected-unattended-shell threat is now explicitly the OWNER'S accepted risk when (and only when) the confirmed toggle is on. | app-only |
| AC109 | **The bridge Enable/Stop choice persists (`bridgeEnabledPreference`) and restores at launch.** `enable()` was the only path that stood up the PQRC node + relay tether host, and it only ran from a Bridge-tab button click — every Huginn relaunch silently dropped the tether until the button was clicked again, which users read as "the tether broke". Internal teardown (enable()'s own cleanup) does not touch the preference; only a user-initiated Stop records OFF. | app-only |

## tech-week — test-suite audit, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]` / `[tech-debt]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC111 | **Full test audit (goal: valid, regression-proof, efficient, no major-functionality gaps).** (a) `RunnerE2ETests.realBinaryHandshakeAndEchoPrompt` now pins `ELDR_ACP_CONFIG_DIR` to a fresh temp dir — it previously inherited the operator's live `~/.config/eldr-acp` (whose `skills`=0 turned it red on a healthy tree). (b) The two Blossom blob-path tests in `PQRCNostr/TransportTests` (round-trip/mirror-failover, constant-size pointer envelopes) are REMOVED with rule 4 — they exercised `BlobCipher`/`LocalBlossomSimulator`, machinery this product will never ship; `ptr` wire-codec fidelity stays pinned in PQRCCore (the NIP still defines it). (c) `GroupTests` fixture serializes mesh establishment through each responder and waits for exact message counts + a 300 ms over-delivery probe: the old over-asked `waitForMessages(4)` burned its full 10 s timeout on green runs (suite 38 s → 1.4 s) and masked a real finding — two initiators racing the same published bundle pick the same `otp.first`, the second `consume` correctly throws `oneTimePrekeyAlreadyConsumed` (one-time = one use), and that link's first message is silently lost (no NACK on a dumb relay). That concurrent-initiation liveness caveat is inherent to dispenser-less relays (a Signal-style server pops OTPs per fetch; a Nostr relay can't) — production recovery is re-initiation against the republished bundle; flagged for THREAT_MODEL. (d) New `App/PQRCTests/LocalMCPGateTests.swift` closes the TEST-PLAN §13 in-app MCP gap (token gate over a real UDS, unconditional codename/64 KB redaction accessors, stop()/weak-model fail-close). (e) `UXVerificationTests` reframed: rec1 pins the shipped AI-control consolidation as regression coverage (portrait before Details — the landscape split view scrolled the wrong container); rec4/rec5 still document live issues (typed note dropped on large-paste send `largePaste ?? draftText`; no passphrase strength floor). (f) ThreadView bubbles now pass the SAME `PartyColor` palette as ConversationView — the nil-palette accent/purple fallback measurably fails the WCAG audit (white on accent gradient); the thread composer got the main composer's padded-background treatment (bare rounded-border was under the 44 pt hit target). (g) TEST-PLAN §13's two "known reds" verified RESOLVED (Huginn `LLMTokenAtRestTests` passes headless; `perAIKeyAccount_matchesLegacyRule` asserts acp has no per-AI key). (h) The `--uitest` universe has a boot race in which conversation seeding occasionally never materializes a single row (zero `conversation-*` elements at 150 s vs rows by ~50 s healthy; all state is in-memory, so it is scheduling, not persistence) — `UXVerificationTests.launchUniverse` mitigates with one relaunch, and a TODO(AC111) marks the seed path in PQRCApp for root-causing. Latent, unfixed: `LocalMCPServer.start()` `.pathTooLong` on iOS-length tmp paths (Catalyst-only feature today, noted in TEST-PLAN §13). | app-only |

## tech-week — solo semantics for the tethered node, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC110 | **The node answers its OWNER's 1:1 chat without requiring a live ai_window (solo semantics), aligning the tether with every other "My AI" surface (C7).** Previously `handleInboundPrompt`/`broadcastAgentMessage` gated ALL `.direct` sends on `isAuthorizedForOwner` — so the solo owner↔node chat went permanently, silently dead the moment the first window lapsed ("pairing works but it never responds", reported twice). Rationale: SPEC §13's window produces the visible indicator that protects OTHER humans in a conversation; an owner-only reply reaches no third party, and the codebase already embodied this rule in TWO places — `sendDraftToOwner` ("no window gate — the draft is private, owner↔agent E2EE") and the phone's own solo My-AI gate class. Boundaries unchanged: C-3 still drops non-owner prompts unanswered; any conversation with a non-owner recipient (groups, threads) keeps the full fail-closed window/invite gate (now with a local DiagnosticsLog note instead of pure silence); sends stay agent-labeled (invariant 8) and E2EE to the owner. Tests: `soloOwnerChatSendsWithoutWindow`, `soloOwnerPromptAnswersWithoutWindow`, `groupStillFailsClosedWithNoOwnerWindow`, and the forged-window test now uses a group so it stays load-bearing. | app-only |

## tech-week — SwiftA2A stays internal; the "official Swift SDK" ambition is dropped, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]` / `[tech-debt]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC112 | **Reverses the extract-and-propose-upstream goal that framed the whole A2A section header and the `Packages/SwiftA2A/Package.swift` preamble ("built to be extracted to a standalone public repo and proposed to the a2aproject as the official Swift SDK").** Re-surveyed the ecosystem on 2026-07-18: two community Swift SDKs already exist. (a) **`tolgaki/a2a-client-swift`** (Apache-2.0, v1.0.5, 23 tags, A2A v1.0, **client-only**) is the Swift SDK actually *linked from the official A2A community page* (`a2a-protocol.org/latest/community/`) — this was missed at adoption time; the AC95–AC101 memo only noted the other one. (b) **`Victory-Apps/a2a-swift`** (MIT, v0.5.0, ~32 commits / 6 releases, A2A v1.0, **client+server**, zero-dep core + optional Vapor/Hummingbird, incl. **Linux**) is architecturally our near-twin — same `AgentExecutor` + `TaskStore` decomposition and the same five security schemes. There is still **no *official* Swift SDK** (Python/JS/Java/.NET/Go are official), so proposing ours was never foreclosed — but with two incumbents (one blessed on the official site) and ours carrying higher deploy targets (iOS 17/macOS 14 vs their iOS 15–16), a macOS-only HTTP binding, and **one commit of history with zero iteration**, ours is a weaker public candidate, not a stronger one. **Decision (owner-confirmed): keep `SwiftA2A` as a private, load-bearing implementation detail; abandon the upstream-SDK ambition.** It earns its place internally — zero-dep, client+server, permanently refuses push notifications (AC95), and is the only implementation carrying the E2EE A2A-over-Nostr transport (`RelayA2ATransport` / `docs/A2A-PQRC-EXTENSION.md`). It is load-bearing in two surfaces: the **client** half (`A2AClient`+`A2ACore`) in `PQRCACP/Sources/A2AHarness/A2AACPBridge.swift`, the **server** half (`A2AServer`+`A2AHTTPServer`+`A2ACore`) in Huginn `A2AServerHost.swift`. **Not adopting a community package:** rip-and-replace is a net downgrade — tolgaki is client-only (cannot replace Huginn's server), Victory is 3★/pre-1.0 with no push-refusal and no Nostr E2EE frames; swapping a load-bearing dependency in an E2EE product for either trades control + auditability for nothing we need. **What remains upstream-worthy is only the *protocol extension*** — the E2EE-over-Nostr profile (`docs/A2A-PQRC-EXTENSION.md`), offered as an extension spec, not the SDK. **Now stale as a result:** the upstream-gated TODOs are de-scoped to internal nice-to-haves — **AC99** ("Required before proposing the SDK upstream" — JWS card verification), the REST-binding / Linux-port gaps, and **AC101**'s "when the SDK is proposed" spec-consistency filing. The `Package.swift` preamble's extraction rationale is now aspirational/stale (the zero-dep constraint it justifies is still worth keeping, for clean layering rather than extraction) — flagged, not yet rewritten. | app-only / tech-debt |

## tech-week — WS-M0: Huginn MLX perf foundation + managed-server kill-switch, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC113 | **WS-M0 lands the MLX tab's perf foundation (publish hygiene + sectioned view) and the owner-requested managed-server kill-switch.** Root cause confirmed in code: ONE `ObservableObject` (`MLXService.shared`) invalidated the whole six-section Form on every streamed job-log line AND every 3 s health probe (written even when unchanged) — and the server-log pane only repainted at all by riding those unrelated publishes (the view never observed the tailer). Changes: (a) `probeStatus` writes only on change (`updateProbeStatus`, also used by the exit/stop/autostart paths); `applyProbe` is internal so the dedupe is pinned by a publish-counting test. (b) Job output is a **buffered feed**: chunks accumulate in `pendingJobLogChunk` and land in `jobLog` in ~4 Hz batches (injectable interval); `LogTailer` batches appended lines the same way (250 ms flush, one `objectWillChange` per batch including the trim, via single-assignment publish). (c) The `suffix(300).map` window projection is hoisted into the service as precomputed `serverLogWindow`/`jobLogWindow` (`MLXLogRow`, stable ids) recomputed once per coalesced publish, and `jobPaneState(for:)` scopes pane state to the section owning the job kind. (d) `MLXView` is split into six Equatable child sections (+ shared `MLXJobPane`/`MLXLogPane`) taking narrow values/bindings; the service is passed as a plain UNOBSERVED reference for actions. Swift-6 wrinkle: `View` is MainActor-isolated in the macOS-26 SDK, so every `==` witness is `nonisolated` and may only read Sendable `let`s — bindings that the section also displays get a value copy taken from the same parent-body read (`currentConfig`, `playModelValue`), which cannot diverge because any change republishes the service and rebuilds the struct. (e) `LogView` rendering bounded to the last 300 lines (Copy-all still copies the full 2000-line buffer) until WS-M2's console replaces it. (f) Found-and-fixed while in the tailer: `stop()`+`start()` (Logs tab hidden/re-shown) re-seeded the tail and APPENDED a duplicate copy of everything already shown — re-open now RESUMES from the prior offset (bounded; rotation/large-gap falls back to a fresh tail seed), and `stop()` flushes pending lines so none are lost across the gap. (g) **Kill-switch (owner ask):** persisted `mlx.managed` (default ON = historical behavior). OFF stops the child/launchd server (launchd: bootout + plist removed; how-it-ran remembered in `mlx.resumeMode`), cancels the 3 s monitor, stops the server-log tailer (zero MLX-tab churn), and hands the Eldr backend to the saved external URL/model pair via the SAME `ConfigurationStore.llmURL`/`llmModel` seam `useAsEldrBackend` writes — seeded from the current values on first use, so "run LM Studio's server on the same port" needs zero edits; the pair persists independently (`mlx.externalLLMURL`/`mlx.externalLLMModel`) and is restored on later OFFs. ON re-saves whatever the user typed while unmanaged, restarts tailer+monitor, rewires the backend to the (independently persisted) MLX config, and resumes per `resumeMode`. `startServer()`/`setAutostart(true)` are inert while OFF (a child spawned with the monitor idle would sit in `.starting` forever). UI: toggle + compact "MLX is off — Eldr uses <url>" card with inline URL/model fields bound to the store; the model library/downloads stay usable (they only need the venv). **Verified:** Huginn suite 117/117 incl. 9 new tests (flood-coalesce with a STRUCTURAL publish bound — elapsed/interval, not a timing assert; resume-no-duplicates; probe-dedupe publish count; buffered-job-feed; pane kind-scoping; backend handoff round-trip; persistence + idle-at-init; child stop→resume; inert-start-while-off). **Not verified here:** Instruments hang-detection before/after (needs the live GUI + real server traffic — owner/on-device); manual flood harness for that session: `i=0; while :; do echo "flood $((i+=1))" >> ~/.config/eldr-acp/mlx/mlx-server.log; sleep 0.02; done`. Step 4 (`@Observable` migration) deliberately NOT taken — it was conditioned on "only if still janky" after on-device verification. | app-only |

## tech-week — WS-B5 retro-record + WS-C/WS-D sweep, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC114 | **WS-B5 (retro-recorded): ONE `SybilclawGatewayClient`, in PQRCACP.** The two copies (`Apps/Huginn/Sources/Services/` and `Packages/EldrNode/Sources/eldr-node/`) — which had already drifted once and broken the path (AC73) — are deleted in favor of a single parameterized client in `PQRCACP` (EldrNode and Huginn both already depend on it; URLSession available in both). Session scoping is a parameter, which fixes EldrNode's cross-conversation single-`sessionBase` bleed (all of an owner's chats bucketing into one gateway session — AC69's scope caveat); an optional diagnostics hook replaces the app-side logging shims. The framing/handshake pin tests moved with the code, so a future drift still fails a test. One "Gateway" settings surface with a flavor picker (sybilclaw primary / OpenClaw optional) collapses the duplicated `ConfigPaths` config-path pairs. Recorded now because the WS-B5 commit (1178ed1) predates this entry — the DEVIATIONS doc lagged the code. | app-only |
| AC115 | **WS-C/WS-D sweep — the phone app's honesty + dedup fixes, and the shared command chips.** (a) **C1 composer:** `sendCurrent()` bumps a `composerEpoch` and arms a 2 s `justSent` guard; the multiline (`axis: .vertical`, UITextView-backed) field's late binding write-back — which restored the just-sent text wholesale and produced the confirmed "message sends but stays in the field, ready to re-send" bug — is recognized in `onChange` (exact restore or whitespace-only remnant, within 2 s, in one jump) and re-cleared; a real keystroke disarms the guard. A `draftWithAI` insertion landing after a send is dropped by the epoch check (the deliberate `mentionDraft` fill is NOT epoch-gated: its content is fresh AI text, not the sent message). Same guard in ThreadView, whose two send paths were factored into one `sendCurrent()`. Regression UI test: send → field empty and stays empty → Send disabled. (b) **C2:** message #0 of `startConversation`/`establishWith`/`startNearbyConversation` and the `reviseRoster` fan-out now mint and carry `messageID` inside the ciphertext (the main `sendMessage` path already did), so both devices key the same message identically; the receiver additionally DROPS a message whose stable id is already stored — making relay+Nearby double delivery and the new auto-resend idempotent. (c) **C3 honest send states:** `PQRCMessenger` send paths return a `DeliveryRoute` (`nearby`/`relay`); the sender's copy is stored as `"sending"` and flipped to `"sent"` (relay hop — the legacy string keeps every stored row and test fixture valid; the BUBBLE is what now renders honestly), `"sent-nearby"` (local link only — no more "Sent to relay" for a Multipeer delivery), or `"failed"`. `publishWithRetry` drops 60→12 attempts (seconds to a visible "Not sent", not minutes of a false "Sent to relay"); recovery is event-driven — transport lifecycle streams (B2) feed a new `.relayReconnected` MessengerEvent and the runtime re-publishes each still-failed send ONCE per recovery (bounded 32-entry FIFO, byte-identical body ⇒ receiver-side stable-id dedup; manual retry/delete clears the entry). The dead `"queued"` bubble branch is gone. (d) **C4:** the ACP approval card moved into its OWN `.alert`-level passthrough `UIWindow` (`ApprovalWindowPresenter`, torn down on silo lock) — a `MainView` overlay rendered UNDER every sheet, which is exactly where users were when requests arrived; the small UIKit window is presentation plumbing, not custom chrome (SwiftUI-only rule kept for UI). The phone now mirrors the node's C-1 timeout: an unanswered prompt resolves `.deny` at 120 s and records a visible "Approval request expired" system row in that node's chat (`recordLocalSystemRow`); arrival fires a haptic + an OPT-IN local notification (per-silo, OFF by default) whose body carries contact name + tool kind only — never the command/path (invariant 12 treats the lock screen as a log). (e) **C5:** pairing ingest (`NewChatView` coding_agent tagging) calls `ensureMacTetheredAIConfigured` immediately, so the tether appears in Settings ▸ AI at pairing time, with consent toggle, autonomy state, approval-notification toggle, and an "open its chat" link on the acp AI's detail (not on the first Details visit). (f) **C6:** ONE `ConversationAIContextSection` (mode picker = the single writer of `conversationContextMode`, live echo, collapsed per-AI `AIInspectionView` with the conversation fixed) embedded by BOTH `ConversationDetailsView` and `AIHubSheet`; the hub's duplicate picker (`ai-hub-context-mode`) and its "go to Settings ▸ AI" pointer row are deleted — the UX test now asserts the shared identifier. (g) **C7:** the Nearby toggle auto-applies (dismiss + `rebootSingle`, the same path the old hidden "Apply & reconnect" took, zero extra taps); Settings shows a live "Nearby now: N peers" count (`MultipeerLinkTransport.verifiedPeerCount`, 3 s poll while visible); the footer states the real prerequisites (both devices ON + app open, verified contact, real hardware — simulators have no radios); the stale "Debug-only" doc comment on `enableLocalLink` is corrected. (h) **WS-D:** `CustomCommand {label, text, autoSend}` lives in PQRCACP (one type, both apps); the phone terminal renders the user's chips first (purple tint; autoSend OFF fills the stdin field) with an inline add/reorder/delete editor persisted per-silo via `AppSession`; Huginn's Test Chat mounts the same strip above its composer (auto-send rides `sendDraft()`), persisted in `ConfigurationStore` and seeded ONCE from the enabled skills (`/spec /snippet /html`, autoSend off — a slash command wants arguments). | app-only |

## tech-week — WS-M1 MLX serve surface, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC116 | **WS-M1: the MLX tab's serve surface — brain card, one-click brain swap, menu-bar controls, port-conflict diagnosis, reasoning toggle, Configuration linkage, environment demotion, cache knobs.** (a) **Plan premise falsified, owner re-decided:** the approved plan's server KV flags (`--kv-bits`/`--kv-group-size`/`--quantized-kv-start`/`--max-kv-size`) DO NOT exist on `mlx_lm server` in ANY released mlx-lm (verified against the venv's 0.31.3, which is also PyPI's latest — they are `generate`-only; emitting them would argparse-kill every server start). Owner picked "server knobs + playground KV": the server's Advanced config gains the cache knobs the server actually has (`--prompt-cache-size`, `--prompt-cache-bytes` behind a GiB field with a trap-guarded conversion — `Int64(Double)` traps past Int64.max and this converts typed text), and the true KV-quantization quartet landed on the Playground's `generate` (gated: group-size/start emitted only when bits is set). Side-correction: the plan's non-goals rationale "speculative decoding doesn't exist in mlx_lm" is stale (`--draft-model`/`--num-draft-tokens` ARE on server 0.31.3); the non-goal itself stands as a scope decision. (b) **Brain card** (`MLXBrainCard`, top of tab): model, state dot + plain-language state, uptime (self-updating `Text(_, style: .relative)` — zero periodic republish), RSS memory (`ps` sampled every 5th monitor tick ≈15 s, published at 16 MB granularity so allocator jitter can't re-render the tab), host:port, and an "Eldr backend" badge when Configuration ▸ Local LLM points at this server. The environment section is DEMOTED into the card: a chip row + ⋯ menu (Check/Update/Reveal) when ready; the full install card auto-appears only while it's needed (not installed / broken / installing / failed install) — the plan's "auto-offer on first use". (c) **One-click brain swap** — `makeBrain(model:store:)` on every cached model row ("Serve as my AI's brain"): validate → config → (re)start → wait answering (240 s deadline, 500 ms poll) → `useAsEldrBackend` → "Your AI now runs on X". Fail-closed at every step: the backend is wired ONLY after health; acceptance additionally requires `launchedServerConfig == serverConfig` (right after a restart the OLD child/probe still reads "answering" for a beat — a review-pass catch that would have reported success while the old model served) AND `answeringServerIsOurs()` — an lsof ownership gate on the answering port (child pid, or `launchctl print`'s pid), because `launchctl bootstrap` returns 0 even when the launchd python then loses the bind race and dies silently, leaving e.g. LM Studio answering "healthy" on the port (adversarial-pass catch: the swap would have wired Eldr to the WRONG server under a green success note; positive foreign evidence fails closed and records the diagnosis, an empty lsof fails open so tooling hiccups can't block a healthy swap). A same-config-already-answering fast path skips the multi-GB reload — full-config equality, not model equality, since a changed port must still restart. (d) **Port-conflict diagnosis** (`portDiagnosis` + `MLXCommand.parsePortOwners`/`portConflictMessage`/`parseLaunchdPID`/`parseEtime`/`parseProcessStats`, all unit-tested): triggered on unexpected child exit (the classic lost-bind-race), launchd unreachable ×3 ticks, launchd answering-with-no-job-pid, and a child starting→healthy imposter verify. Renders plain language: "Port 1337 is held by LM Studio (pid 843) — stop it there or change the port here." (e) **Reasoning toggle**: `MLXServerConfig.reasoning: Bool?` → Model default / On / Off Picker emitting `--chat-template-args {"enable_thinking":…}` only when set (the sampling-flags version-guard pattern); a one-time `absorbingReasoning` migration pulls the owner's hand-typed flag out of Extra arguments (unspaced, spaced-JSON — the live server's exact form — and `=` forms; richer JSON is left alone, and extras stay LAST in argv so a kept duplicate still wins in argparse). (f) **Menu bar**: `MLXMenuBarSection` (Equatable child) with dot + model + state, Start/Stop, and an "MLX tab" jump via the new `MainWindow.openTab` notification (tab selection was private `@State`; the Configuration chip uses the same jump). KNOWN LIMIT: with the main window fully closed, activate cannot resurrect it — the same pre-existing flaw as the menu bar's "Open Eldr node". Observing `MLXService.shared` from the menu bar means the service (and its 3 s monitor) now starts at app LAUNCH rather than first MLX-tab visit — deliberate: a launchd-managed server must show live status without opening the tab; kill-switch OFF still idles everything. (g) **Configuration ▸ Local LLM** gains a leaf-observer "Managed by the MLX tab" chip (edit-where-you-inspect; MLX churn re-renders only the chip row). (h) All new published state is dedupe-guarded per WS-M0 discipline. **Verified:** Huginn suite 138/138 (21 new tests: reasoning/prompt-cache/KV emission + gating, absorb migration ×6, lsof/etime/ps/launchctl parsers, GiB trap-guards, legacy-config decode compat, brain-swap fail-fast ×3 — invalid model side-effect-free, kill-switch refusal, missing-env — and init-absorb persistence). Swift Testing gotcha recorded: `#expect(optionalTuple?.member == literal)` can fail with IDENTICAL displayed values (Int64-vs-Int inference inside the macro's type-erased compare) — unwrap with `#require` and type the literal. **Not verified here (owner, on-device):** the live two-model swap while Test Chat stays up — the live :1337 production brain is a DETACHED process Huginn doesn't own (bouncing it kills the working tether mid-use), and the HF cache currently holds only ONE mlx_lm-loadable model (the GGUF + NVFP4 entries can't load); download any small MLX build to run the acceptance swap. | app-only |

## tech-week — WS-M2 log console, 2026-07-18 — `[app-only]`

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC117 | **WS-M2: one reusable log console (search, markup, remediation, back-scroll) replaces LogView and the MLX server-log pane.** (a) **Architecture:** pure logic in `LogStore.swift` (`LogMarkup` classify+AttributedString styling, `LogSearch` compile-once query model, `LogRemediationCatalog`, `LogBackscroll` line-aligned older-chunk reads + bounded on-disk stream search) — all unit-tested headlessly; live state in `LogConsoleModel` (one per console surface); UI in `LogConsoleView` (`.embedded` compact pane / `.full`), expanded sheet, and a `WindowGroup(for: LogConsoleSource.self)` "Open as window" scene. Sources: mlx-server.log / eldr-acp.log / the DiagnosticsLog bus (severity rendered AS a level token so one markup path styles everything; rebuilds gated on visibility). **Each console owns its own visibility-driven LogTailer** rather than reusing `MLXService.serverLog` — the service tailer's lifecycle belongs to the kill-switch + failure scan, and coupling the user's viewing to it would either undermine the OFF-state churn promise or stop the scan; two tailers on one file are two fds, harmless. `MLXService.serverLogWindow` (WS-M0) is superseded and removed; `jobLogWindow`/`MLXLogPane` stay for the inline job panes. (b) **Rows accumulate past tailer retention** (cap 10 000; tailer keeps 2 000): the model appends per publish by id, so scrollback outlives the tailer window; a `fileGeneration` bump (rotation / oversized stop-start gap — new LogTailer signal) resets the accumulation because it no longer joins up. (c) **Search:** debounced 250 ms, plain case-insensitive/sensitive or real regex (invalid → flagged field, zero-width matches dropped), match count + wraparound next/prev, current match followed BY IDENTITY across appends/prepends, highlights via AttributedString background (current match distinct), searching suspends follow-tail. Embedded pane renders its 300-row streaming window EXCEPT while searching — an adversarial-pass catch: match navigation would scrollTo unrendered ids (silent no-op) while the count claimed hits. (d) **Back-scroll + disk search (full style):** "Load older" prepends 64 KB line-aligned chunks (negative ids; seam anchored bottom so the loaded chunk is what appears; cap-refusal + no-progress retire the affordance honestly with a Finder hint), and a token-guarded off-main stream search of the on-disk region before the anchor reports "N older matches — load & jump" (walks chunks to the earliest match, bounded 128×64 KB = the 8 MB scan window, lands selection on the oldest match). Known honest limits: >8 MB-older regions are unscanned (`truncated`), and a 10 k-row head-trim disables further back-scroll (offset of the new first row is unknown — a prepend would silently gap). Stale-async discipline: chunk results are dropped unless source AND anchor still match (offset equality alone is not identity — a switched source's fresh anchor can coincide); disk-search results carry a token. (e) **REAL BUG FOUND BY THE SEAM TEST, fixed in LogTailer:** a 64 KB seed of short lines exceeds the 2 000-line retention cap; `publish` trimmed the head AFTER ingest, leaving `earliestSeedOffset` pointing ~4 000 lines below the first retained row — "Load older" spliced history in with a silent gap (`line-14042 then line-18000`). Seeds are now bounded to the cap IN DATA SPACE (`tailLineStart`, physical-newline walk immune to the empty-line/display-line mismatch) so the anchor is exact; regression tests at both layers. (f) **Remediation chips:** most-specific-first catalog (mlx_lm module missing / CacheNotFound / HFValidationError / no-safetensors wrong-format / address-in-use / Exception-in-thread), latest occurrence wins scanning the recent 200 rows, dismissal per failure class (a DIFFERENT failure re-arms). (g) **Privacy (inv. 12):** the console renders existing local tool logs only; Copy-all goes to the user's pasteboard on their action; no new sinks. `LogMarkup`'s regex statics are `@MainActor` because `Regex` isn't Sendable in this SDK — every ingest site is main-isolated anyway. (h) **Verified:** Huginn suite 176/176 (38 new: markup/classify, search model incl. zero-width + invalid regex, remediation order + latest-wins, chunk chaining reaches BOF exactly once, disk-search offsets/carry/truncation, tailer anchors + generation + overlong-seed cap, model accumulation past retention, clear/switch/diagnostics/chip lifecycle, search position stability, seam contiguity, disk-search jump, flood publish batching ≤15 for 50 writes). Launch-verified on this Mac; live :1337 brain untouched. **Not verified (owner, on-device):** Instruments hang-report while flooding with search open, and the sheet/window feel at desk. | app-only |
| AC118 | **WS-M3: model-library polish — real download progress + free-disk guard, format honesty, per-model detail popover, cache-hygiene sort.** Premises re-verified against the live venv (mlx-lm 0.31.3, hf_hub 1.24.0); one was FALSE (the AC116 lesson). **(a) Download-output reality:** the plan's "percent/rate parsed from the tqdm line" implied a byte bar — captured by running the app's EXACT `snapshot_download` command through a non-TTY pipe, hf_hub 1.24.0 emits a **file-count** bar (`Fetching N files: P%|…| x/N [el<rem, rate]`) and SUPPRESSES per-file byte bars off a TTY. So `downloadProgress` is files-based and the rate is files/s, and the `ProgressView` is captioned in tqdm's own wording, NOT a fabricated MB%/MB-rate. `parseTqdmProgress` anchors on the percent immediately preceding the bar pipe (`P%|`, so a bare "50%" in prose can't false-match) and reads the rate as the numeric token after the last comma in `[…]` (absent on the `?it/s` opening frame); the service picks the newest tail frame via pure `latestProgress`. 4 real frames + the 0%+unauth-Warning concatenation frozen as fixtures. The plan's example id `mlx-community/SmolLM2-135M-Instruct-4bit` DOESN'T EXIST (401) — used `mlx-community/SmolLM2-135M-Instruct` (273.9 MB, loads at 0.318 GB peak), the 2nd loadable MLX model WS-M1's NEEDS-OWNER wanted here (manual-download acceptance = this capture). **(b) Format truth** (read the 0.31.3 loader): `load_model` requires `model*.safetensors` and there is NO GGUF path in generate/server/utils (GGUF cannot load); `compressed-tensors` is force-mapped to affine 4-bit, which can't unpack NVFP4 (`unsloth/…-NVFP4`: has safetensors, arch `qwen3_5` = SAME as the working brain, no `mlx` tag). `classifyFormat(id:tags:)` → `mlx` tag = loads; gguf tag/name = cannot; nvfp4 tag/name = cannot; else = "not an MLX build"; the copy distinguishes "can't load" (GGUF/NVFP4) from "not an MLX build — prefer the mlx-community equivalent". NVFP4-cannot-load is code+plan-inferred, **NOT re-verified by a live 27B load** (OOM risk beside the resident 15 GB :1337 brain). **(c) Free-disk guard** (AC103f, before the job): `volumeAvailableCapacityForImportantUsage` on the cache volume + best-effort true size from the HF tree API (sum of file sizes; `usedStorage` over-counts — 75 GB vs ~23 GB — and was rejected); the pure `downloadDiskGuard` blocks only when free < size+margin, warns-not-blocks on unknown-size-and-low-disk, never blocks on a nil probe. `downloadModel` became async via `beginDownload` (network size lookup off the button; job exclusivity re-checked after the await). **(d) `lastUsed`** = max blob atime captured in the SAME `scanModels` walk that sums size (atime verified live on this APFS volume: +1 s on read; the working model's blob atime > mtime from the server's load); nil → "—". `quant` from the snapshot `config.json`; nil → "—" (adversarial-pass catch: nil ALSO covers an unreadable config, so "full precision" would over-claim). **(e)** `downloadProgress` is dedupe-guarded and `.download`-scoped (set in `flushJobLog`, reset in `startJob`, cleared in `finishJob`); `MLXModelsSection ==` gains it so only that section re-renders on a progress tick (WS-M0); the library stays fully usable with `mlx.managed` OFF (only "Serve" is gated on `managesServer`); no new payload log sinks (inv. 12); `cacheDir` made an injectable init param (like `launchAgentsDir`) so a test never scans the user's real HF cache. **REAL REGRESSION the full-suite run caught (loop 1):** the first full run red-flagged 6 keychain/token tests; proven via git-stash that it was MINE, not environmental — clean HEAD full = green (those 6 pass), my tree = red, and the 6 pass in isolation on HEAD. The Huginn keychain tests have a **pre-existing parallelism sensitivity** (concurrent `securityd`/SecItem access under Swift-Testing parallelism → transient `load == nil` after save); a WS-M3 test that spawned a subprocess (the download-wiring test) tipped it (a first patch — empty `cacheDir` + echo-exit — passed once then FAILED again next run, proving the subprocess itself was the trigger, not the cache scan). **Fix:** WS-M3 tests now spawn ZERO subprocesses — the wiring test was replaced by the pure `latestProgress` unit test; the frame-picking logic is fully covered without a child process. Suite then green twice (197/197). Pre-existing keychain-parallelism flake is flagged for a future test-infra cleanup (serialize the keychain suites) — out of WS-M3 scope. **Adversarial-pass catches (loop 2):** the quant "full precision" over-claim (→ "—"); integer-overflow traps in `downloadDiskGuard`'s margin add + `sumTreeDownloadBytes`'s running sum on hostile/absurd network sizes (→ `addingReportingOverflow`, clamp-and-block, matching the AC116 trap discipline); a force-unwrap in the atime walk; and the subprocess-flake root cause above. **Verified:** Huginn suite 197/197 (twice, stable) incl. 21 new tests (tqdm parser incl. locale/no-bar/impossible-percent + latest-frame pick, format classifier, disk guard + tree-sum + overflow, quant-label + sort + scan-populates-lastUsed/quant). **Not verified (owner, on-device):** ProgressView/popover/sort feel at desk; the free-disk BLOCK path (112 GiB free — pure-tested only); the NVFP4 live load. | app-only |
| AC119 | **WS-M4: teachable fine-tune — Guided/Expert modes, dataset assistant, live loss chart, memory preflight, after-run try/fuse.** New `FineTuneGuideView.swift` (Guided default: base → data → preset; Expert keeps every knob, each with in-place `.help` = what-it-does · safe range · symptom-of-wrong); `MLXFineTuneSection` becomes a narrow Equatable wrapper. Premises re-verified against the installed venv (mlx-lm 0.31.3); one FALSE (the AC116/AC118 track). **(a) Loss/checkpoint format — plan STALE:** `Iter N: Train loss …` does not exist in 0.31.3. Captured from a REAL tiny LoRA run through a pipe: the `mlx_lm.lora` trainer uses a rich-console UI (`cli_ui.py`) that emits ANSI SGR color EVEN off a TTY. Real lines (ANSI-stripped): train `<iter> <loss> ▼/▲ <tok/s> <tokens>k`, val `<iter> val <loss> <time>s`, save `save <NNNNNNN>_adapters.safetensors`. `parseLossLine` strips ANSI with a state machine (no Regex — runs on every log line: `[` after ESC introduces CSI, params continue, a 0x40–0x7E final byte ends it) then reads iter+loss; the ▼/▲ trend arrow marks a train row. `parseLossHistory` rebuilds the curve per coalesced flush, published only on a NEW point (sparse — well under 4 Hz), reset ONLY when a NEW fine-tune starts (loop-2 catch: an unrelated download/generate must not wipe the last run's chart). Real lines frozen as fixtures. **(b) CLI/YAML:** `lora` fine_tune_type choices [lora, dora, full] — DoRA EXISTS (`tuner/dora.py`); keys verified in CONFIG_DEFAULTS; `loraConfigYAML` gained save_every / steps_per_eval / max_seq_length / grad_checkpoint; `full` omits the lora_parameters block. **(c) Dataset formats** (read `tuner/datasets.py`): precedence completions (prompt+completion) > chat (messages) > text; files train/valid/test.jsonl. The assistant offers the schemas, `validateDataset` (a sample-validator mirroring that precedence, reporting **line + kind ONLY, never record content** — SPEC §0 / inv. 12), a CSV→completions builder (quoted-comma aware) + train/valid splitter; it NEVER reads chat history / conversation stores / encrypted memory — only files the user explicitly picks. **(d) `generate --adapter-path` CONFIRMED** (the "Try with adapter" premise) — the trained adapter made SmolLM2 answer "Widget 3 is amber." (a fact only the synthetic data taught); the after-run flow fills the Playground with base+adapter (shared `playAdapter` binding) and pre-fills guided Fuse. **(e) Memory preflight:** available RAM = `host_statistics64` free+inactive+purgeable+speculative × `getpagesize()` (a `mach` call, no subprocess; `getpagesize()` NOT the `vm_page_size` global var, which fails Swift-6 strict concurrency), LABELED an estimate everywhere; peak = base × (1.3 LoRA/DoRA · 4.0 full) + 2 GB (overflow-safe clamp — AC116/AC118 trap discipline); verdict comfortable/tight/risky/unknown; "stop the server during training" offered ONLY for a Huginn-managed server — a detached/foreign server (e.g. the :1337 brain) shows up as pressure Huginn can't control (implicit in available RAM). **ACCEPTANCE RUN (dev-time, the plan's requirement):** `python -m mlx_lm lora --config ft.yaml --train` on mlx-community/SmolLM2-135M-Instruct, 30 iters · batch 2 · 4 layers · LoRA rank 8 · lr 1e-4 · steps_per_report 5 · steps_per_eval 10 · save_every 10. Trainable 0.242% (0.326M/134.515M). Val loss 4.073 (it1) → 0.803 (10) → 0.203 (20) → 0.264 (30) — it learned; adapters + 0000010/20/30 checkpoints saved; exit 0, seconds of compute; :1337 untouched. **Perf/discipline:** loss chart is its own Equatable child; guided/expert @State-driven; the fine-tune section re-renders only on its inputs; jobs exclusive (AC103f); preflight is a button, before the job; WS-M4 tests spawn ZERO subprocesses (the WS-M3 keychain-parallelism lesson). **Loop-2 adversarial catches:** dataset-count copy over-claimed a sampled file's total (→ "records checked / first N / a sample"); the CSV import claimed success even when the `try?` write failed (→ do/catch); the loss chart was wiped by any job start (→ scoped to fine-tune); `estimateTrainingPeakBytes` could `Int64(Double)`-trap on an absurd base (→ clamp); and the loraConfigYAML test was strengthened to pin LINE STRUCTURE (the plain contains-checks were blind to a multiline trailing-newline / concatenation trap — verified NOT present, now pinned). **Verified:** Huginn suite 210/210 (green twice) incl. 13 new tests (ANSI strip + real train/val/save parse + history/dedup + trend; presets→YAML + full-omits-lora + line structure; dataset precedence + line-only errors + split/CSV + templates; memory estimate/verdict + overflow). **Not verified (owner, on-device):** the guided flow / loss chart / dataset assistant / memory card at desk; a full GUIDED-mode LoRA run through the app UI (the CLI acceptance run proves the pipeline; the UI wiring is unit-tested). | app-only |
| AC120 | **Adversarial review pass over WS-M3 (AC118) + WS-M4 (AC119) — 10 findings, 9 fixed, 1 non-issue proven.** The two workstreams were implemented by a delegated two-loop agent; this row records the independent hostile review of both diffs. **WS-M3 fixes:** (a) **"Last used" self-poisoning (the headline catch):** `scanModels` reads each model's `config.json` for the quant label (via the snapshot symlink → blob), which bumps that blob's atime — and `lastUsed` was max-atime over ALL files, so from the second refresh on, every model's "last used" would equal "when the library last refreshed". `directoryUsage` now tracks atime over WEIGHT-CLASS files only (≥ 1 MiB: weight shards, tokenizer blobs — what a real load actually reads); models with only small files honestly report nil ("—"). Regression test: a small-files-only cache entry must yield `lastUsed == nil`. (b) **Download preflight dead air:** the disk/size check (an HF tree fetch, up to ~10 s) ran with no UI state — the tap looked dead and a second tap raced into a misleading "another task is running". New dedupe-guarded `downloadPreflight` publish: caption shown, Download disabled, duplicate taps dropped. (c) The low-disk `.warn` message outlived a SUCCESSFUL download as a stale-looking error — cleared on success iff still the same message (a real failure message is left alone). **WS-M4 fixes:** (d) **`availableMemoryBytes` double-counted speculative pages** — xnu's `host_statistics64` already folds them into `free_count` (PROVEN on this Mac: free_count − speculative = 8597 ≈ vm_stat's "Pages free: 8579"), so `+ speculative_count` biased the OOM preflight toward optimism, the exact wrong direction. Term removed. (e) **CRLF line-splitting was broken at the String layer, worse than a trim bug:** Swift's `split(separator: "\n")` does NOT split CRLF text — "\r\n" is ONE grapheme cluster — so a Windows-origin train.jsonl reached the validator as a single "line" ("not a JSON object") and a CRLF CSV became one mangled row with \r\n baked into the JSON. My first fix (trims only) was itself proven insufficient by the new tests failing; the real fix is `splitLines` (LF / CRLF / lone-CR separators) used by the validator + CSV builder, trims kept as belt-and-suspenders. (f) **CSV rows were dropped silently** (one-column lines, multi-line quoted cells) while the result note claimed clean success — `completionsFromCSV` now returns `skippedRows` and both the too-few-rows error and the success note state the count and the rule (two non-empty columns per line; multi-line cells unsupported). (g) **`validatePicked` read the whole picked file** (`Data(contentsOf:)` — a multi-GB train.jsonl would balloon memory): new `boundedSample` reads ≤ 4 MiB and cuts at the last complete line so truncation can't fake a corrupt record; the note says "a sample" only when actually sampled. (h) **The after-run card read the LIVE form fields**, so editing the form after a run made "Try with adapter"/"Fuse this adapter" load a base+adapter pair that was never trained together — the started run's pair is now captured (`lastRunModel/Adapter`) and offered. (i) **Loss-trend turnaround:** a val curve of 4.0→0.5→0.9 read as "falling — learning" under first-vs-last; an interior-minimum check now reports "bottomed at L around iter N and rising — likely overfitting; prefer the checkpoint nearest iter N" (val series only, ≥ 3 points). (j) The Fuse button with empty fields started a guaranteed argparse-failure job (pre-existing, not an agent regression) — disabled until model/adapters/save-to are set. **Non-issue proven (no change):** the LR field's `.number` format was suspected of rejecting the scientific notation its own placeholder suggests — probed: `FloatingPointFormatStyle<Double>.number` parses "1e-4"/"1E-4" fine. Also re-confirmed: the recurring SourceKit diagnostics on `MLXSupport.swift` (bare regex literals parsed as division) are single-file indexer noise, not compiler errors. **Verified:** Huginn suite 214/214 (210 + 4 new review tests: CRLF CSV + skipped count, CRLF validator/splitter, overfit turnaround, bounded sample; the CRLF tests were written first, failed against the agent's code, and pass after the fix — the review's proof-of-bug discipline). **Not verified (owner, on-device):** all AC118/AC119 NEEDS-OWNER items stand; the preflight caption + skipped-rows copy are desk-check items. | app-only |
| AC121 | **WS-M5: workshop demotion + the three approved AC riders + docs hygiene — the MLX overhaul's last workstream.** (a) **Demotion (owner decision, plan §WS-M5):** Convert/quantize + Playground move behind a persisted "Advanced" disclosure at the BOTTOM of the MLX tab (`mlx.advancedExpanded`, default collapsed) — code fully intact, both sections unchanged inside, demotion only. Resulting order: Brain card / Server (which owns the WS-M2 console) / Library / Fine-tune / Advanced — the plan's sketch said "Brain card / Library / Console / Fine-tune / Advanced", but the console lives INSIDE the server section since AC117 and re-plumbing it out is riskier than the demotion ask; recorded as an interpretation, not silent drift. The mtime heuristic stays as the second, weaker signal. (c) **AC94 rider:** `HarnessDescriptor.withCommand` (`.stdioSpawn`-only copy-modifier, mirroring `withVendorKey`) + `HarnessRegistry.commandOverrideEnvVar(for:)`/`resolvedDescriptor(id:environment:)` in PQRCACP; the CLI's `delegate_to_cloud_agent` now resolves through `resolvedDescriptor`, so `ELDR_HARNESS_CMD_<ID>` (id uppercased, `-`→`_`) pins the executable for GUI/launchd-spawned nodes whose PATH lacks nvm/npm dirs — the exact operational caveat the registry already documented. Huginn side: per-id overrides in UserDefaults (injectable `defaults` on `ConfigurationStore` — a path is not a secret), applied in BOTH `resolvedHarnessDescriptor` variants (before secret merges), MIRRORED into the agent env file on every write so both halves stay in lockstep, and edited in the Bridge tab: an "Executable" field (registry default as placeholder) + Browse panel with hidden files shown (nvm lives in dot-dirs) + a not-executable orange warning + honest captions. (d) **AC104 rider:** the Bridge "Agent project folder" box gains "Spilled tool results: <size>" + a confirmed destructive Clean that deletes `<workdir>/.eldr/tool-results` (path built from `ToolExecutor.spillDirRelative`, made public so UI and executor can never drift; size via the tested `HFCache.directorySize`, computed off-main). Scope limit recorded: cleans the CURRENT configured workdir only — prior workdirs' spills are not tracked anywhere and stay the operator's. (e) **Docs:** stale T5 corrected in place (relay chunking shipped in N26/A25; Blossom rejected permanently; the entry claimed the opposite), and the SwiftA2A `Package.swift` extraction preamble rewritten to match AC112 reality (internal SDK; official-Swift-SDK ambition dropped). **Verified:** PQRCACP `swift test` green (264 tests / 49 suites + the A2AHarness product's 8 — includes 5 new `HarnessCommandOverrideTests`); Huginn suite 215/215 (new `HarnessOverrideStoreTests` pins defaults→resolution→env-file round-trip incl. clearing); the convert smoke run above. **Not verified (owner, on-device):** the Advanced disclosure / Try-auto-open feel; a real delegation spawn THROUGH a pinned override (the resolution seam is unit-tested end-to-end, but no live cloud-CLI turn was driven here); the spill Clean against a real spill dir; the version-mismatch warning against a genuinely stale binary. | app-only |
| AC122 | **Open-source release prep — phases 0–1 of the OSS plan (2026-07-19).** (a) **Split licensing decided (owner):** the eight `Packages/` are **Apache-2.0** (embeddable in proprietary products, express patent grant, matches swift-crypto), the app targets (`App/` EldrChat, `Apps/Huginn`) and everything else **AGPL-3.0-only** with an App Store additional permission under AGPL §7 (`LICENSE-EXCEPTIONS.md` — the "Signal-style" framing in the plan was dropped: Signal-iOS's LICENSE was checked and is plain AGPL with no such exception, so the attribution was false; the shipped wording is the widely-used app-store additional-permission formulation); map in `LICENSING.md`; SPDX one-liners on all 322 non-manifest Swift files (the 8 `Package.swift` excluded — the tools-version declaration must stay on line 1). Contribution terms: DCO for packages, CLA for the app targets (preserves commercially licensed app builds, e.g. a future Huginn Pro). This REPLACES §15.2's blanket-AGPL recommendation and the BSD-3 sketch in the (now-private) business notes. (b) **SPEC reconciled to the shipped stack:** §15.1 rewritten (Swift-native `PQRCNostr` + `swift-secp256k1`, khatru anchor relay, swift-crypto CVE pin — the drafted rust-nostr/UniFFI + Blossom stack never shipped); §11 gains a binding status note (Blossom path NOT implemented, permanently rejected — T5 / AC111(b); §11.2 chunking is the only >64 KB path; `ptr` stays wire-defined for forward compat).
