(d) **Export:** EXPORT-COMPLIANCE gains the §742.15(b) open-source notification step (email BIS/NSA the public repo URL at publish). Remaining before publish (tracked in the OSS plan): author-email history rewrite + full-history scrub gate, gitleaks gate, fresh public remote. | app-only |
| AC123 | **`ELDR_PCC_SDK` was hardcoded ON, making the repo unbuildable on every stable toolchain and on CI — found by the fresh-contributor dry run, fixed as an environment opt-in (2026-07-19).** `Packages/PQRCAgent/Package.swift` carried `.define("ELDR_PCC_SDK")` unconditionally while the comment directly above it insisted the flag "is OFF by default … It is therefore OPT-IN" — the comment described the intent, the code contradicted it. **Impact (proven, not inferred):** `swift test --package-path Packages/PQRCAgent` on stable Xcode 26.6 fails with `PCCFoundationModelsProvider.swift:200: cannot find type 'ContextOptions' in scope`, because the PCC FoundationModels symbols ship only in the Xcode 27+ SDK. Since the entire iOS app imports PQRCAgent, this broke **3 of the 5 CI jobs** (`packages` matrix, `app-build`, `app`), and it is **unfixable by selecting a different CI Xcode**: GitHub-hosted `macos-26` runners offer 26.0.1/26.1.1/26.2/26.3/26.4.1/26.5/26.6 and no Xcode 27 at all (verified against the live `actions/runner-images` manifest). It went unnoticed locally because CLAUDE.md mandates building with the beta's `DEVELOPER_DIR`, which masks it — the defect is only visible from a fresh contributor's toolchain, which is exactly what the OSS plan's dry-run step exercises. **Fix:** `Package.swift` now computes `let pccEnabled = ProcessInfo.processInfo.environment["ELDR_PCC_SDK"] != nil` and applies the define conditionally, implementing what the comment already specified. PCC is NOT disabled — the owner gets it with `export ELDR_PCC_SDK=1` alongside the beta `DEVELOPER_DIR`, and both are now documented in CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING, and the README. **Verified both directions:** stable Xcode 26.6 with the variable unset → PQRCAgent 84/84 green; Xcode 27 beta with `ELDR_PCC_SDK=1` → builds with the PCC path compiled in. Guard comments in Package.swift and CLAUDE.md warn against re-adding the unconditional define, since the failure mode reads like a missing API rather than a build-configuration bug. | app-only |
| AC124 | **History rewrite + fresh-remote plan ABANDONED; repo publishes in place with history intact (owner decision, 2026-07-19). Supersedes AC122's closing sentence.** AC122 listed "author-email history rewrite + full-history scrub gate … fresh public remote" as remaining pre-publish work, and that rewrite was fully rehearsed in a scratch clone (git-filter-repo: 249 commits re-authored to the GitHub noreply address, all name/email/team-id/codename hits eliminated from every blob, gitleaks clean, rewritten tree still built and tested green). **It is not being applied.** `github.com/SnoobieJunes/Eldr` goes public *in place* — same remote, same 245-commit history, same commit hashes — because a fresh repo throws away the public record for no benefit the owner values. **The distinction that matters, and the reason the working-tree scrub is RETAINED:** a `git clone`/`git pull` of the current code must not hand anyone the owner's personal identifiers, so the tree keeps its scrub (no `Created by <name>` source header, no `/Users/<name>/...` doc path, no `<name>@mac.local` test fixture, `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM` blanked in both pbxproj, trademarked codename retired from every title). History deliberately keeps all of it — real authorship email, pre-scrub blobs, the Team ID, the old codename — because concealment was never the goal: *"if people want to go look they can see I'm not hiding anything."* **Consequences accepted, not oversights:** (a) pre-scrub content is one `git show` away; (b) the six branches already on `origin` carry pre-scrub working trees, so pruning the stale ones before flipping visibility is the one cleanup that actually changes what a casual browser sees (runbook step 2); (c) the trademark survives in history — tolerated because the current tree and every public-facing title are clean, which is what a complaint would target. **The full-history scrub-term grep gate is therefore DROPPED** (it only had meaning for a rewrite); the gitleaks gate stays and passes on the real, unrewritten history (245 commits, 56 findings, all verified false positives, allowlisted with reasons in `.gitleaks.toml`). | app-only |
| AC125 | **The `ELDR_PCC_SDK` flag is DELETED; PCC now detects the SDK itself. Supersedes AC123 (2026-07-20).** AC123 replaced a hardcoded `.define("ELDR_PCC_SDK")` with an environment opt-in — correct for CI, and it introduced the opposite defect: **Xcode launched from the Dock does not inherit shell environment**, so the app the owner actually builds and runs never saw the variable and shipped with PCC compiled out. The device reported *"This build was compiled without the Private Cloud Compute SDK"* on an iPhone that fully supports PCC. A flag-file fallback (`.pcc_enabled`, gitignored) was tried and failed the same class of test: invisible in the manifest, not part of SwiftPM's manifest-cache key, and still a per-machine step someone must remember. **The root problem was the existence of a switch at all**: hardcoded-ON breaks every stable toolchain and CI (AC123), opt-in breaks the owner's own GUI builds (AC125), and a single flag can only ever pick one of those failures. **Fix — no flag, in either direction.** The gate is now the SDK's own module version, in exactly one place (`PCCFoundationModelsProvider.swift`): `#if canImport(FoundationModels, _version: 2.0)`. Xcode 27's FoundationModels declares `user-module-version 2.0.59` and vends `PrivateCloudComputeLanguageModel`/`ContextOptions`; Xcode 26.6 declares `1.5.2` and vends neither — **verified in the iPhoneOS, iPhoneSimulator and MacOSX `.swiftinterface` of both toolchains**, which also retires the AC25 concern that the macOS seed lacked the symbols (the 27.0 macOS SDK now has all 47 decls, so Huginn is safe). `Packages/PQRCAgent/Package.swift` carries no PCC settings at all. **Verified, both directions, with `ELDR_PCC_SDK` unset and no flag file present:** Xcode 27 → PQRCAgent 87/87 green and the PCC-only test `pccSDKPresent_neverReportsMissingSDK` *executes* (it exists only inside the PCC branch); Xcode 26.6 → green with the *other* branch's test `pccSDKAbsent_degradesWithActionableReason` executing instead. **Proven end-to-end in the shipped artifact:** `xcodebuild build -scheme EldrChat` on Xcode 27 with a clean derived-data path → `BUILD SUCCEEDED`, and the resulting app binary contains every PCC-branch-exclusive string (quota-reached, unreachable, not-eligible, systemNotReady) plus a linked `PrivateCloudComputeLanguageModel` symbol, while the "compiled against an SDK without…" string is **absent** — i.e. PCC is genuinely in the binary, not merely configured. **New regression guard:** `PCCBuildGateTests` (the first test coverage this gate has ever had — both prior regressions shipped precisely because nothing asserted it) asserts the behavior correct for whichever SDK compiled it, so it stays green on 26.x *and* 27 while failing loudly if the gate breaks. A future 26.x point release that shipped PCC at version `1.6` would read as a false negative → graceful Demo fallback, never a build break; the failure direction is deliberate. CLAUDE.md carries a **do-not-touch** section, as this failure reads like a missing API rather than a build-configuration bug and has now been "fixed" wrongly twice. **Platform scope (added after adversarial review):** the gate is `#if canImport(FoundationModels, _version: 2.0) && (os(iOS) || os(macOS))`. The `os()` clause is not cosmetic — Xcode 27's FoundationModels module declares version `2.0` on tvOS/watchOS/visionOS as well, so the version check ALONE opens the gate there, but PCC is `@available` only for iOS/macOS/visionOS/watchOS 27 and `unavailable` on tvOS, and this file's `@available(iOS 27, macOS 27, *)` guards resolve `*` to the deployment floor (26) on the other platforms → a hard compile error. The old `ELDR_PCC_SDK`-off-by-default masked this (the code never compiled on those platforms); auto-opening the gate is what made it reachable, so the scoping was added in the same change. Verified by typechecking the gate pattern against the watchOS/watchsimulator/tvOS/visionOS SDKs: version-only errors on all four, version+`os()` compiles the degraded branch on all four; Mac Catalyst (`os(iOS)`) and native macOS both build the real PCC path. This product ships iPhone/iPad/Mac only, so the scope is exact; a future watchOS/tvOS target degrades to the "no PCC API" message rather than breaking. **Honest limits (not fixed here, recorded so they aren't mistaken for guarantees):** (1) the gate is version-based, not symbol-based — a hypothetical future SDK that bumped the module to `2.x` but dropped a PCC symbol would break the build rather than degrade; acceptable because the alternative (per-symbol `#if canImport` probing) doesn't exist in Swift. (2) `canImport(_version:)` fails OPEN for a module with no `user-module-version` stamp at all (an unversioned module satisfies any requested version); inert today because Apple stamps it, but the failure direction there is toward a build break, not a fallback. (3) The regression the owner hit is *re-based, not eliminated* — PCC is still off when you build with a 26.x toolchain (that SDK genuinely can't compile it); the win is that it is now off **only** because of the SDK, with no hidden variable to forget, and it can no longer differ between `xcodebuild` and Dock-launched Xcode on the same SDK. (4) `PCCBuildGateTests` guards the build gate, not runtime availability — over-claiming (green "Active" in Settings when the managed entitlement is unapproved) is a separate, pre-existing runtime concern that needs a real iOS 27 device to exercise and is untestable on any current machine/CI. | app-only |
| AC126–AC136 | **Recorded in the "gooseworld" section at the end of this document** — cross-town transport, the goose ACP harness, the town wall, and standing town grants. The numbers were reserved on `main` so they stayed stable across the branch merge; they are now filled in. | see below |
| AC137 | **The protocol is severed from the copyleft and dedicated to the public domain (CC0-1.0) — amends AC122's blanket "everything else is AGPL".** AC122 licensed `Packages/` Apache-2.0 and *everything else* AGPL-3.0-only, which swept in the specification, the NIP, the extension profiles, and the interop test vectors. That was wrong in three concrete ways, all found in a licensing audit (2026-07-22). (a) **The NIP was un-upstreamable.** `nostr-protocol/nips` states "all NIPs are public domain"; an AGPL document cannot be submitted there as-is, and `NIP-XX-pqrc.md` already declares upstream submission as its intent. (b) **`docs/A2A-PQRC-EXTENSION.md` declares "Intended venue: submission to the a2aproject community as a registered extension"** — a2aproject is Apache-2.0 and can no more accept AGPL text than an Apache-2.0 Nostr client can accept AGPL code. The stated contribution strategy was blocked by our own LICENSE. (c) **An internal contradiction:** `TestVectors/` was AGPL yet is read by Apache-2.0 test code (`PQRCCore/Tests/.../VectorSupport.swift`, `PQRCNostr/Tests/.../TestSupport.swift`), so anyone taking `PQRCCore` at its word got a test suite they could not legally run — and interop vectors are pointless unless *rival* implementations may check themselves against them. **Legal grounding, not just preference:** under 17 U.S.C. §102(b) (and *Baker v. Selden*) copyright does not reach a "procedure, process, system, [or] method of operation", so the AGPL never actually prevented anyone from implementing PQRC from the SPEC — it only deterred them, conferring no protection while costing adoption; and under *Feist* (499 U.S. 340) the vector values are facts compelled by the algorithm, close to uncopyrightable. **Fix:** `docs/pqrc-SPEC-v1_1.md`, `docs/NIP-XX-pqrc.md`, `docs/A2A-PQRC-EXTENSION.md` carry `<!-- SPDX-License-Identifier: CC0-1.0 -->`; `TestVectors/LICENSE` carries the CC0 text (a file, not per-file headers — the vectors are frozen byte-for-byte and JSON has no comment syntax, so **no `.json` was touched**); canonical text in `LICENSES/CC0-1.0.txt`; SPEC §15.2 and the front-matter line rewritten; `LICENSING.md` and README re-cut around the rule **"anything another implementation needs in order to interoperate is public domain; the reference code is permissive; the shipped apps are copyleft."** Sole authorship verified by `git log` on every relicensed path, so no third-party copyright had to be cleared, and AGPL→CC0 is a *loosening* — safe before or after publication. **Split verified sound:** Apache-2.0 is one-way compatible with AGPL-3.0 and every dependency arrow points the legal way — packages depend only on each other plus swift-crypto/swift-asn1 (Apache-2.0) and swift-secp256k1 (MIT); zero `AGPL` tags in `Packages/`; zero app-module imports in `Packages/`; 120 app→package imports; all 8 package suites green (536 tests) after the change. `LICENSING.md` also gains that compatibility rationale, the missing swift-asn1 attribution, and an explicit statement of what the AGPL does *not* do. Note: CC0 §4(a) waives no trademark or patent rights, so this is compatible with AC138. | app-only |
| AC138 | **`TRADEMARKS.md` added — the actual anti-clone lever, because the AGPL is not one.** The owner's goal ("prevent people from just cloning our apps and distributing them") does not follow from copyleft: the AGPL expressly *permits* forking, rebranding, and commercial redistribution, requiring only that complete corresponding source be published (§13 for network use). It is friction, not prohibition. What stops a rebadged clone shipping beside the original is **trademark**, which neither license conveys — Apache-2.0 §6 disclaims it outright, AGPL §7(e) permits reserving it, and CC0 §4(a) explicitly does not waive it. The repo had no naming policy at all, despite the owner having already retired the previous codename under AC122 *because it was someone else's mark*. The new policy reserves **Eldr**, **EldrChat**, **Huginn**, the logos, and `eldr.app`; explicitly permits nominative fair use (*New Kids on the Block*, 971 F.2d 302) and independent implementation; and restricts **naming only** — never use, study, modification, or redistribution — so it removes nothing from the open-source grants and leaves OSI compliance intact. The ask reduces to: *fork it, rename it, ship it.* Unregistered marks are enforceable under Lanham Act §43(a) (15 U.S.C. §1125(a)) but are geographically limited to areas of actual use (*Tea Rose-Rectanus*); **federal registration under §1051 remains the recommended next step** — it grants nationwide constructive use from the filing date (§1057(c)), incontestability after five years (§1065), and, practically, turns an App Store clone takedown into a form submission rather than an argument about common-law rights. A source-available non-compete license (BUSL-1.1 / PolyForm / Elastic v2) was considered and **rejected**: it would forfeit open-source status and contradict the Nostr/a2aproject contribution strategy. Owner confirmed 2026-07-22 that no patents are held, so the absence of a patent covenant on the CC0 documents is a non-issue. | app-only |
| AC139 | **The Linux `eldr-node` port (WS-L*), 2026-07-23.** The GOOSEWORLD-LINUX-PORT plan is implemented far enough that a Linux `eldr-node` **compiles, holds its identity, and dials the relay** — verified in a local Apple-`container` swift:6.2 VM (offline-seeded SwiftPM cache; no second machine). Six one-commit-per-phase changes behind platform guards, macOS/iOS provably byte-identical (every change under `#if canImport(Darwin/CryptoKit/Network/os)` / `os(Linux)` / `os(macOS)`; all five package suites green). **WS-L1/G3** — compile-unblock: CryptoKit→Crypto shims, `OSLogShim` (no-op-on-Linux Logger keeping invariant-12 privacy), FoundationNetworking/`os` guards, a Linux `objCType` JSON bool/int/double discriminator (CoreFoundation type IDs are Apple-only; runtime-validated), `LLMClient.stream` non-streamed Linux fallback, Glibc socket-type fixes for the gooseworld/MCP binaries, and the `eldr-acp`/`eldr-acp-run`/`pqrc-relay` macOS-only executables stubbed on Linux. **Zero-dependency exception (deliberate):** PQRCACP links swift-crypto's `Crypto` and EldrNode links `CryptoExtras` **only on Linux** (`.when(platforms: [.linux])`) — on Apple the zero-external-package promise holds; unavoidable because Linux has no CryptoKit/Secure Enclave (SPEC §2 "no invented primitives"). **WS-L2/L3 — invariant-10 platform decision (on par with AC31):** `IdentityStore` seam; a Linux `FileIdentityStore` (AES-256-GCM per-account 0600 files, `HKDF(masterKey, account)`) whose master key rides a ladder — **TPM-sealed via systemd-creds** (`$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY`, no in-process TPM code) → **scrypt-passphrase** KEK (`KDF.Scrypt` from swift-crypto's CryptoExtras, RFC-7914-KAT-verified, N=2¹⁶/r=8/p=1 — so **no new crypto dependency**; the port doc's "swift-crypto has no memory-hard KDF" was wrong). `KeystorePosture` is DERIVED from the rung that ran, reported loudly; a TPM present-but-unsealed is refused unless `ELDR_KEYSTORE_ALLOW_NO_TPM=1`; a systemd credential is only labelled hardware-backed when a TPM is actually present (`.systemdCredential` otherwise); a half-present keystore fails closed rather than reminting identity. **WS-L5 — the relay transport:** `URLSessionWebSocketTask` is non-functional on swift-corelibs ("WebSockets not supported by libcurl"), so a Linux node dials `wss://` over **SwiftNIO** (`NIOWebSocketChannel` + NIOSSL, `NIONostrTransport` reusing the shared `NostrWire` NIP-01 codec) — the proven Apple `NostrWebSocketTransport` is untouched; swift-nio/-ssl are Linux-conditioned so the iOS app never links them. Frame cap 1 MiB + fragment aggregation (relay events exceed the 16 KiB default), an upgrade-timeout so a non-101 can't hang, a keepalive ping (Cloudflare idle-close), per-operation redial (the messenger assumes it), and a fail-closed AUTH verdict check. **WS-L0** — a Linux CI job (build + keystore/NIO test suites), green-by-construction. **Accepted limits / DEFERRED:** live `wss://` against the real relay is device-gated (no container network); **WS-L4 (agent tool-host + PTY re-seam) is NOT done** — porting the ~1400-line `ACPAgent`/`ToolExecutor`, `openpty` (absent from Swift's Glibc module → needs a C shim), and the A2A-harness coupling is a fresh effort; only the cross-town DELEGATE/coding plane needs it, not the wall plane. A Fable-5 3-pass audit found + fixed real WS-L5/keystore bugs (frame size, fail-open AUTH, no-reconnect, connect-hang, partial-corruption remint, TPM overclaim) before this row was written. | tech-debt |

## gooseworld — WS-G1–G4: cross-town transport, goose harness, wall + standing grants, 2026-07-20

Eldr as the secure channel between goosetowns (`private/GOOSEWORLD.md`): a peer town's
agent can send/receive over the same PQ-E2EE, gift-wrapped, Double-Ratcheted mesh a normal
chat rides, every agent stays cryptographically bound + labeled, and every autonomous
cross-town exchange sits under a human-signed, visible, revocable authorization. The four
protocol/transport workstreams below are implemented and green on the packages; the app-layer
surfaces (Huginn grants panel, phone indicator chips) and the live two-machine relay proof
(Phase 0) are the remaining, device-gated work and are NOT claimed here.

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC126 | **Standing town grants (`standing_grant` / `standing_grant_revocation`) — a first-class human-signed authorization for day-scale cross-town autonomy.** SPEC §13 gates every autonomous agent send on a human-signed `ai_window` (hours-scale) or a thread `ai_invite`. A multi-day two-town co-build (GOOSEWORLD §5) fits neither — windows lapse mid-build (the AC110 "tether went silent forever" failure mode) and per-task approval is unusable on a wall with hundreds of posts. Rather than widen the §13 gate per surface (which is how invariant 9 erodes), a first-class, human-identity-signed **standing grant** is added: day-scale, scoped to one peer town identity + plane(s) (`wall`/`delegate`), with per-UTC-day message/byte budgets, a concurrent-task ceiling, and an optional tool allow-list. Its own signature domain (`pqrc-standing-grant-v1`), its own revocation object and domain (`pqrc-standing-grant-revocation-v1`); every scoped field is bound into a **length-prefixed** canonical `scopeTag` so a grant cannot be reflected onto another peer, plane, or budget (a first-draft `planes.joined("+")` was caught allowing `["","wall"]` to collide with `["+wall"]` — a signature-transplant escalation — and replaced, with a tombstone test). Bounded is mandatory (`maxStandingGrantDuration` = 30 days; allowed 1/3/7/14/30); an unbounded grant throws. Enforced by a **separate** engine entry point (`AgentEngine.authorizeTownSend` / `beginTownTask` / `recordTownSend`), **never** by `authorizeAutonomousSend`, which is byte-identical to before — a test asserts that holding a standing grant does NOT make an ordinary window/thread send succeed. Budgets are message-driven (evaluated from `clock.now()` at call time, never a timer; day rollover is monotone against a backwards clock — invariant 1), consumed by explicit `recordTownSend`. Revocation is effective on receipt, clears local state via `defer` even if signing throws, only removes grants whose granter is the revocation's signer, and treats an unknown grant id as a silent no-op (no state-probe oracle). Live grants expose expiry + remaining budget (`activeStandingGrant`/`activeStandingGrants`) so every client can render an indicator for the grant's lifetime (invariant 9). Wire fields ride INSIDE the ciphertext as optional `MessageBody` members, omitted when nil — frozen `TestVectors/` are byte-identical after the change (SPEC §12). **Verified:** PQRCCore 96/96 (+14), PQRCAgent 116/116 (+29), frozen vectors unchanged, downstream PQRCNostr 114/114 unbroken. Full wire format + validation rules: `docs/pqrc-ext-standing-grants.md`. | upstream-NIP |
| AC127 | **WS-G2: `goose acp` added as a VERIFIED ACP harness (`.stdioSpawn`), not scaffolding.** New `HarnessRegistry` row `goose-acp` (`command: "goose"`, `args: ["acp"]`, `isProvisional: false`) driving Block/AAIF goose 1.37.0 in its ACP-agent mode. Verified the WS3d way — a real `initialize` handshake driven through the production path (`runHarness` → `StdioHarnessTransport` → `runACPProxy`), NOT a hand-typed shell probe — and goose returned a well-formed ACP result: `protocolVersion: 1`, `agentCapabilities` (loadSession/prompt/mcp/session), one `authMethods` entry `goose-provider`. **A configured provider is NOT needed to complete `initialize`** — proven by re-running under an empty `HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (no `config.yaml`), which returned the byte-identical result; goose advertises the provider requirement via `authMethods` (the correct ACP shape), not by failing initialize. Still UNVERIFIED (stated in the descriptor header): a real `session/prompt` turn (needs `goose configure` / a provider credential) and `session/new` on an unconfigured install. `vendorKeyEnvVar` is deliberately `nil` (goose owns its own config/keyring — no single env var to inject; declaring one would make the Keychain lookup act on a lie). PATH caveat bites goose harder than the npm CLIs: its installer drops the binary in `~/.local/bin`, off a GUI/launchd default PATH → set `ELDR_HARNESS_CMD_GOOSE_ACP` to the absolute path (the `acp` arg survives the override). Direction note: we are the ACP *client* and goose the *agent* — the SETUP-GUIDE caveat was about the reverse. The live handshake test is `.enabled(if:)`-gated on goose being resolvable, so it SKIPS on CI rather than passing vacuously; **on this machine it RAN and passed** (transcript captured). PQRCACP 267/267 + 8/8. | app-only |
| AC128 | **WS-G1: the cross-town A2A task plane is wired into `EldrNodeCore.serve` behind its OWN authorizer, deny-all by default.** `RelayA2ATransport` was built and unit-tested but wired into NOTHING (GOOSEWORLD's §2 table called the Task plane "Shipped" — optimistic; the transport existed, the integration did not — the table is corrected). `A2A1|` is now routed as a fourth frame class alongside `ACP1|`/`MCP1|`, following the `RelayMCPTransport` pattern. **The design pivot:** a cross-town delegation arrives from a peer town — a NON-owner by definition — so it must NOT reuse the C-3 owner check (which would either kill the plane or, widened, hand every stranger the code-execution-adjacent ACP plane). New seam `TownAuthorizer` (`func authorizes(peerIdentityHex:) async -> Bool`) with a pinned-allowlist impl (`PinnedTownAllowlist`) and a `DenyAllTownAuthorizer` default; plus `TownA2AService` (nil default) for what services an admitted line. Both `serve` params default OFF, so a node with no town config is **byte-identical** to before — no A2A frame admitted, no transport allocated, no existing call site changed. The gate is five sequential fail-closed refusals (is-A2A → service-exists → sender-identified → authorized → peer-table-has-room, `maxTownPeers=8`), a per-peer transport allocated only AFTER authorization so a reply can only go back to the town that asked, and authorization consulted PER FRAME (never cached) so a revoked peer stops being admitted mid-line. Everything reaching the plane stays untrusted remote input (GOOSEWORLD §4 class 1): the C-1 permission gate and C-2 path jail are unchanged, no bypass. **Explicitly NOT a §13 grant** — a config pin is not human-signed/time-bounded/visible; the doc comments say so and mark the seam where AC126's `standing_grant` plugs in. `PinnedTownAllowlist` matches case-sensitively (consistent with C-3's `==`): non-canonical entries dropped at construction, case-variant inbound hex denied — fail-closed both directions. The pre-existing C-3 `EldrNodeServeTests` (owner-only ACP/MCP) pass VERBATIM. **Verified:** EldrNode 28/28 incl. 18 new WS-G1 tests + a relay-carried proof that a real stranger's byte-identical, decrypting A2A frame is dropped while the pinned town's is serviced. | app-only |
| AC129 | **WS-G3: the `eldr-gooseworld` goose extension — a dependency-free MCP surface for cross-town coordination, with injection containment as the load-bearing property (GOOSEWORLD §4 class 1).** New in `Packages/PQRCMCP` (kept at ZERO package dependencies, by design): a pure `TownWall` model, an `UntrustedDataEnvelope` renderer, a `GooseworldBridge` seam + `DemoGooseworldBridge` double, a `GooseworldMCPServer` exposing exactly four tools (`world_wall_post`, `world_wall_read`, `world_delegate`, `world_towns`), and the `eldr-gooseworld` stdio shim (loopback+token, mirroring `pqrc-mcp-bridge` — carries no keys/logic; agent identity stays on the node, SPEC §13.5). **gtwall was READ, not guessed:** the actual 401-line `gtwall` bash script was fetched from the goosetown repo and its semantics documented as verified facts in `TownWall.swift`, with three deliberate divergences recorded — (1) cursors are **monotonic sequence numbers**, not gtwall's line numbers, so a truncated/rebuilt wall can never rewind a cursor and replay old posts as new (gtwall's silent bug); (2) the log is **bounded** (retained-post cap, per-post byte cap, per-read byte cap) because gtwall's unbounded append-only file is a memory-exhaustion DoS in a daemon fed by remote peers; (3) eviction past a reader's cursor produces a **counted, reported gap**, never a silent skip. **The point of the workstream:** `world_wall_read` delivers remote content only as quoted DATA inside a four-layer envelope — (a) per-read 128-bit nonce markers generated after fetch and never posted back, (b) every content line `> `-quoted after splitting on ALL Unicode line breaks so no post line can be a structural line even if the nonce leaks, (c) ANSI/C0/C1/DEL and Trojan-Source bidi controls escaped to visible `<U+XXXX>` (zero-width joiners deliberately preserved — structural in legit emoji/scripts, can't break containment), (d) header fields rebuilt from struct data and re-sanitized, so a body claiming `priority-for-human: yes` or forging a header/marker changes nothing. Containment is a TESTED property: payloads exercised include "ignore previous instructions", verbatim envelope markers, fake system messages, forged priority, ANSI/bidi/control chars, each line-separator forging an END marker, multi-line fake envelopes. `world_delegate` is the code-execution-adjacent plane and **fails closed** — no standing grant → tool error, no override arg; AC126's grant object is left as a documented seam and not referenced. A refused action is always `isError: true` (never a silent drop); no `resources/*` surface exists, to prevent a cursor-free unframed read path. The existing chat MCP surface (`MCPServer`/`SecureChatBridge`/`pqrc-mcp`) is untouched. **Verified:** PQRCMCP 90/90 (15 pre-existing `MCPServerTests` unchanged + 75 new: TownWall 25, envelope 19, server 31), clean build zero strict-concurrency warnings, `eldr-gooseworld` builds and fails-closed on missing config. **Not verified (node-layer, later):** the socket byte-pump is a line-for-line copy of the shipping `pqrc-mcp-bridge` (builds + fails-closed) but has no in-repo node host to run a live loopback session against yet. | app-only |
| AC130 | **WS-G4→G1 integration: `StandingGrantTownAuthorizer` closes the seam AC128 left open.** `TownAuthorizer.swift` (AC128) explicitly named the conformance it could not write while the grant type was being built concurrently. With AC126 landed, that conformance now exists in `EldrNodeCore`: a `TownAuthorizer` whose answer comes from the live set of human-signed `standing_grant`s (PQRCCore's value type — no new dependency; EldrNode does NOT gain a PQRCAgent dep) rather than a static operator pin. This is the §13-shaped authorizer where `PinnedTownAllowlist` is only config: it admits a peer's A2A frames iff the OWNER currently holds a valid, unexpired grant naming that peer on the `.delegate` plane. It re-checks the three properties that can lapse between issuance and an inbound frame — expiry (from an injected clock, `> now`), the owner's signature (re-verified: a tampered expiry/peer fails), and plane coverage (a `.wall`-only grant does NOT open the delegation channel). The load-bearing fail-closed: an optional **granter pin** means only grants the owner signed admit a peer, so a peer that self-signs a grant naming itself — or a third party's grant — is refused (no self-authorization, the confused-deputy C-3 exists to stop). `liveGrants` is a closure re-invoked per frame, so revocation (modeled as the grant disappearing from the set) bites on the very next frame with no cache. Budgets/task-ceiling are NOT enforced here (admission ≠ budget grant) — those stay on the sending side (`authorizeTownSend`). **Verified:** EldrNode 41/41 (28 + 13 new adversarial cases: expiry exact/over, revocation-by-absence, wrong peer, wrong plane, self-sign, third-party sign, tampered expiry, tampered peer, decoys, empty inputs). | app-only |
| AC131 | **In-process TWO-TOWN E2E: the substitute for the GOOSEWORLD Phase-0 live two-machine run (there is no second Mac).** WS-G1's live proof (two machines over relay.lerants.com) is unrunnable, so the cross-town Task plane is otherwise unverified end-to-end. This lands that proof in one process: two full towns over ONE `LocalRelaySimulator`, driving the REAL `EldrNodeCore.serve` loop with the REAL `StandingGrantTownAuthorizer` over a REAL human-signed `StandingGrant`, and — the piece nobody had wired — the initiating town's client-side receive path (`TownAInbox`: its messenger stream → `isA2AFrame` filter → `RelayA2ATransport.deliverInbound` → `inboundLines`). **Semantics confirmed by reading `serve`, not guessed:** admission is governed by the grant Town B's OWNER signs naming Town A on the `.delegate` plane (the authorizer's `requiredGranterHex` pins the granter to the node owner, so a peer self-signing is refused — no self-authorization); this is NOT C-3 (owner-only), which is left untouched for `ACP1\|`/`MCP1\|`. The headline closes the loop: Town A delegates a bead → `serve` admits it via the grant-backed authorizer → the service returns a `role:"agent"`-labeled A2A response (invariant 8) → it rides the per-peer transport back to Town A, decoded BYTE-EXACT; an ungranted stranger's byte-identical bead (a verified contact whose frame decrypts) is dropped. Negatives, all through `serve`: no grant, expired grant (injected clock), wall-only-vs-delegate, wrong peer, and revocation-mid-session (drops on the very next frame — the per-frame, uncached consultation). Crown jewel: a peer holding a LIVE `.delegate` grant still cannot reach the coding plane — a byte-valid `ACP1\|` frame from it (which decrypts at the node) is dropped by C-3, `CountingLLM` turn count stays 0 (confused-deputy → RCE, GOOSEWORLD §4 class 1). Non-vacuity is proven, not assumed: the headline FAILS when the grant is re-pointed at the wrong peer (mutation-tested), and revocation/crown-jewel carry in-run positive controls. **What this does NOT prove vs a real two-machine run:** no live relay (network partition/latency/NIP-42 AUTH/khatru chunk limits untested), no second OS/Keychain, both messengers in one address space, Town B's service is a minimal well-formed A2A responder rather than a live goose flock, and the SEND-side day-budget gate (`AgentEngine.authorizeTownSend`) is out of scope here (PQRCAgent isn't linked into the EldrNode test target — it stays covered by `StandingGrantGateTests`). It is the strongest proof possible without a second machine, and closes everything except the physical two-host substrate. **Verified:** EldrNode 48/48 (41 + 7 new), plus PQRCAgent `StandingGrantGateTests` 29/29 for the cited send-side coverage. No production bug found. | app-only |
| AC132 | **`eldrctl found-town` (GOOSEWORLD §6 WS-G6, town-in-a-box).** Adds a `found-town` subcommand that SSH-provisions a gooseworld town: it reuses `ConduitProvisioner`'s `install-huginn.sh` verbatim for the eldr-node install (golden-file test still byte-identical), then runs a new `FoundTownProvisioner.townScript` that installs the `eldr-gooseworld` goose extension and writes a goose registration stanza, and emits a **town invite** (`pqrc:town?npub=…&hub=…`, a distinct scheme from the `pqrc:add?…&type=coding_agent` coding-agent pairing link so the scanning client routes it to the standing-grant/town-pairing flow, §5). Judgment calls: (1) **hub == relay** — GOOSEWORLD §3 makes the hub a relay, so `--hub` is the single wire the node dials for both the owner conduit and town traffic (`--relay` accepted as alias). (2) **found-town does not auto-install goose** — it verifies `command -v goose` and fails closed with a pointer to goose's official installer rather than fabricate a download-and-pipe-to-bash for an unverifiable URL (same provisional/verified honesty as `HarnessDescriptor`). (3) **the goose config stanza is best-effort/`PLACEHOLDER`** (goose's schema is version-dependent), dropped beside goose's config, not merged. (4) **the town invite is derived controller-side** from the npub the node prints via `--print-pairing-link` (eldr-node has no `--print-town-invite` yet — a documented EldrNode follow-up); the **QR is a display concern**, emitted as a link the scanning surface renders (no new QR-encoder dependency). Secret-free: the gooseworld pairing token is minted by the node and never appears in the generated script or argv (`townScriptHasTheRequiredShapeAndNoSecret`). Fails closed on a bad owner (reused conduit validator), a metacharacter-carrying hub (`isSafeHubURL`), a non-`[a-z0-9_-]` provider (`isSafeProvider`), and `-oProxyCommand=…`-style target injection (reused `isSafeSSHDestination`). **Verified:** Eldrctl 22/22 (10 conduit unchanged + 12 new). NOT run live over SSH (needs a target Mac) — the SSH plumbing is the same proven `sshRun`/`scp` the conduit path uses. | app-only |
| AC133 | **Linux eldr-node port plan (`docs/GOOSEWORLD-LINUX-PORT.md`, WS-G6 follow-up).** A plan-only doc, grounded in real file:line: EldrNodeCore's *logic* is DI'd/platform-agnostic and the crypto core + messenger port with small changes (PQRCCore/PQRCNostr are on `import Crypto`/swift-crypto/swift-secp256k1; `EncryptedStore.MasterKeyWrapper` and `NodeMessenger` are DI seams; `eldr-gooseworld` is already `#if canImport(Glibc)` and ports today). **Corrects the earlier optimistic "EldrNodeCore ports free" claim:** the serve loop hard-binds to the `#if os(macOS)` agent host (`ACPProxy.swift:74`), so it compiles only where macOS does. Top blockers, each with file:line: (A) the node identity store is concrete macOS Keychain, no seam (`NodeKeychain.swift:3` `import Security`, `:37/:55/:68` `SecItem*`); (B) the agent tool-host is `#if os(macOS)` and `serve` hard-calls it (`ACPProxy.swift:74`, `ACPTransport.swift:59`, `PTYProcess.swift:23` `import Darwin`); (C) two stray `import CryptoKit` in the PQRCACP library pulled transitively by EldrNodeCore (`ACPMetadataCrypto.swift:2`, `ACPEvents.swift:2` — one-line `→ import Crypto` swaps). Invariant-10 keystore ladder on Linux = SE→TPM 2.0→Argon2id-passphrase-KEK (the passphrase rung carrying the AC31 deniability/brute-force cost; needs an Argon2 dependency — swift-crypto has no memory-hard KDF, and SPEC §2 bans hand-rolling one). Staged plan + risk/effort table included; nothing compiled on Linux yet, so all portability claims are labelled `[unverified]`. | tech-debt |
| AC134 | **Gooseworld injection-containment audit + WS-G5 headless data-path seam.** Adversarial audit of the cross-town wall containment (GOOSEWORLD §4 class 1). Found and fixed **one real breakout**: `world_towns` rendered a paired-town **label outside** the `> `-quoted envelope while neutralizing only LF/CR, so a raw U+2028/U+2029 (caught by neither the C0/C1 nor the bidi escape rule) **forged an extra roster row** claiming another town was `delegate: granted` — a trust-state forgery via remote-influenced data. Fix: `UntrustedDataEnvelope.singleLineField` collapses the full Unicode line-break set before escaping; the roster and the demo bridge's invalid-id echo route through it. Confirmed already-solid and now tombstoned: full `Bidi_Control` coverage (12/12); FS/GS/RS (Python `splitlines` boundaries) escaped not passed raw; the per-read 128-bit nonce is never persisted to the wall; oversize posts refused (never truncated) at the 64 KB inline ceiling; cursor logic has no silent skip/re-read and is `UInt64.max`-overflow-safe. WS-G5 headless pieces landed: `WallChunking` — a transport-free `{id,index,total}` chunk/reassemble seam mirroring invariant 4's `MessageBody.chunk`, fail-closed on any incomplete set — plus an end-to-end remote-post→read→reply round-trip and a chunked >64 KB finding proven contained through the full server path. The `GooseworldBridge` seam is sufficient and correctly minimal (no MCP-reachable remote-author forgery — `post()` stamps LOCAL); real transport, fan-out, NIP-11 chunk sizing, cursor persistence, and standing-grant verification remain node/app-layer work (PQRCCore/PQRCNostr-dependent). **Verified:** PQRCMCP 90→110, all green; chat MCP surface unchanged; `eldr-gooseworld` builds. | app-only |
| AC135 | **Adversarial hardening of the standing-grant STORE — bounded against a verified-peer memory DoS (found in a hands-on read of the grant engine, the one track no subagent covered).** `AgentEngine.receiveStandingGrant` admitted a grant from any VERIFIED contact with no bound; a paired-but-hostile peer could sign grants naming arbitrarily many distinct `peer` hexes (each a fresh dictionary key) and grow `standingGrants` without limit — a memory-exhaustion primitive fed by another person's machine, the exact unbounded-append class the cross-town wall (AC129) is bounded against, left open in the grant store. Fix: (1) `pruneExpiredGrants` on every `store` — an expired record authorizes nothing already (every gate/query checks `now < activeUntil`), so dropping it is behavior-preserving and keeps the store tracking LIVE grants; (2) a per-granter cap `maxStandingGrantsPerGranter = 64` — a NEW grant id past the cap throws `standingGrantLimitReached`, while a re-issue (top-up) of an existing id is always honored and never counts against it. Fail-closed: refusing a new grant can only ever withhold authorization, never widen it; 64 is far above any real co-build. The same read confirmed the rest solid and non-vacuously tested: granter-keying blocks a peer from granting MY agent send-authorization (invariant 9), `grantKey` is delimiter-collision-safe, `Budget.validate` rejects negatives + caps every field, `validateStructure` pins peer to 32-byte lowercase hex, cross-replay across all four signed types is tested (`domainSeparation_noCrossReplayAmongAllFourTypes`), and `authorizeAutonomousSend` is proven byte-identical (`the §13 gate is NOT widened`). **Verified:** PQRCAgent 121 green (the 5 tests named below), PQRCCore 96 green, frozen vectors unchanged. **Correction (2026-07-22):** this row originally claimed those +5 tests as done when the tree contained none of them — the bound shipped with a coverage claim and no coverage, caught by a full-suite re-run that reported 116, not 121. The tests now exist in `StandingGrantGateTests`: `grantStoreIsBoundedPerGranter_newIDPastTheCapIsRefused` (typed refusal at the cap + nothing partial stored), `reIssueOfAnExistingGrantIDIsHonoredAtTheCap` (top-up adds no key, so renewal still works when full), `expiredGrantsArePrunedAndFreeTheGranterQuota` (prune is what stops the cap becoming a permanent lockout; carries a positive control), `theCapIsPerGranter_soOnePeerCannotLockOutAnother` (the bound contains the attacker, not the node), and `theCapCountsGrantIDsNotPlaneRecords` (a both-planes grant costs one unit, not two — counting records would silently halve the bound). | app-only |
| AC136 | **Relocatable Huginn models folder (the HF hub cache can be pointed anywhere).** The MLX tab always stored downloaded models in the default Hugging Face cache (`~/.cache/huggingface/hub`); the owner keeps theirs elsewhere (`Development Projects/models/`). Added a persisted relocation: `MLXService.customModelsDirKey` (UserDefaults, not a secret), `resolvedCacheDir(defaults:env:)` (nonisolated + pure — user relocation wins, else the standard `HF_HOME`/`HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE`/default), and `setModelsDirectory(_:)` which persists, re-resolves `cacheDir`, mkdirs it, and rescans. `cacheDir` went from `nonisolated let` to `@MainActor private(set) var` (the one detached scan already snapshots it, so no cross-actor read — verified by a clean build). **Fixes a latent read/serve split in the same change:** the `mlx_lm` server and the download job spawned with the app's inherited env only (a GUI/launchd process never exports `HF_HOME`), so they used `~/.cache` even if the scanner pointed elsewhere; both now spawn with `mlxSubprocessEnvironment`, which pins `HF_HUB_CACHE`/`HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE` to `cacheDir`. UI: a "Change…"/"Use Default" control in the Models section (reuses the existing `pickFolder`), with a "restart the server to load from here" caption (the running server reads the cache env only at launch — no auto-restart, to avoid racing the port teardown). **Verified on-device:** `xcodebuild test` for Huginn → TEST SUCCEEDED, incl. 4 new `MLXModelsDirectoryTests` (default fallback, custom-wins-and-tilde-expanded, blank-ignored-so-env-applies, key-stability) + the full pre-existing MLX suite green. | app-only |
| AC140 | **Hermes (Nous Research) wired into the ACP + MCP surfaces — no protocol code, both directions reuse existing seams.** Question: can someone configure Hermes against our servers, or is new functionality needed? Answer, from the code: neither seam needs protocol work. (1) **ACP (we are the client, Hermes the agent):** one data row in `HarnessRegistry.all` — `hermes-acp` / `command: hermes` / `args: ["acp"]` — exactly the goose-row drop-in the registry is designed for; nothing in the spawn/proxy/permission path changes and it appears in Huginn's picker + the phone's router by itself. **Deliberately `isProvisional: true`** (owner's call): Hermes is NOT installed on the dev machine, so this matches the Codex/OpenCode/Cursor unverified rows, not the WS3d/WS-G2 verified ones. `vendorKeyEnvVar: nil` for goose's exact reason — Hermes sources providers from its own `~/.hermes/config.yaml`, so no single env var to inject and declaring one would make the Keychain lookup act on a lie. `hermes acp` needs Hermes' optional `[acp]` extra; the AC94 `ELDR_HARNESS_CMD_HERMES_ACP` override handles the uv/pipx PATH hazard (the `acp` arg survives). Tests: `HermesHarnessTests` — always-run registry/override assertions + a `.enabled(if:)`-gated live `initialize` through the production path (`runHarness`→`StdioHarnessTransport`→`runACPProxy`) that SKIPS here (no `hermes` on PATH) and lights up the moment it's installed, so the row can never pass vacuously. (2) **MCP (Hermes is the client of our walls):** `MCPServer` is already client-agnostic. The one real gap — `hermes mcp add --command` has no documented way to pass env vars, but `pqrc-mcp-bridge`/`eldr-gooseworld` fail closed without their socket+token — is closed the way the ACP launchers already close it: `MCPClientLauncher` writes an env-injecting wrapper (`~/.local/bin/eldr-mcp-chat` / `eldr-mcp-town`, 0700) that sources a 0600 env file (holding the secret) and execs the shim. **The wrapper is secret-free by construction** (AC132's `found-town` precedent) because `hermes mcp list`/config dumps would otherwise print a live pairing token; the env file is written only if absent, so re-install never clobbers a pasted token. New `ConfigPaths` accessors (`mcpBridgeLauncher`/`mcpBridgeEnvFile`/`mcpBridgeShim` + the town trio; env files in configDir, not the PATH-listed binDir, so the secret isn't where clients enumerate executables). Tests: `MCPClientLauncherTests` — wrapper shape (sources env, execs shim), 0700/0600 modes, no token-env-var name in the wrapper, and re-install preserves the user's env file. **MCP revision:** no code change — `MCPServer.knownVersions` already advertises up to `2025-11-25` and echoes its `2024-11-05` default otherwise (spec-compliant graceful fallback); if a newer Hermes pins a revision past that, adding it is a one-line echo-list edit. Docs: SETUP-GUIDE §9 "Hermes — both directions"; iOS Settings snippet left untouched (already client-agnostic). **NOT VERIFIED — the honest status:** no claim about Hermes' own behaviour has been run — that `hermes acp` speaks the ACP dialect our client drives, that `hermes mcp add --command` accepts the wrapper, and which MCP revision it requests are all open; the row stays provisional until a live run. **Verified here:** registry/override + wrapper unit tests, all 8 package suites + Huginn green. | app-only |
| AC141 | **WS-L4 — the ACP agent tool-host runs on Linux (2026-07-24; closes the one AC139 deferral).** A Linux `eldr-node` now hosts the FULL coding agent — the cross-town DELEGATE/coding plane, not just the wall. **Gates widened** to `os(macOS) \|\| os(Linux)`: `ACPAgent`, `ToolExecutor`, `runHarness`/`runACPProxy` (ACPProxy), `runACPAgent` (ACPTransport), `StdioHarnessTransport`, `DefaultHarnessTransportFactory`, `ACPClientDriver`'s `.spawn` path, and the `eldr-acp`/`eldr-acp-run` executables (stubs removed; `FoundationNetworking`/`Glibc` imports added). **`NodeShell`** picks the node shell per platform — `/bin/zsh` on macOS, `/bin/bash` on Linux (both accept `-lc` and `-l -i +m`) — one constant shared by `run_shell`, the PTY spawn, and the tool description, so they cannot drift. **PTY re-seam:** AC139's C-shim prediction was RIGHT — Swift's Glibc module exports none of the pty family and hides `POSIX_SPAWN_SETSID`, so the header-only internal target `CEldrPTYShim` exposes `eldr_openpty` and the REAL glibc `POSIX_SPAWN_SETSID` (0x80 — Darwin's is 0x0400; reading real headers instead of hand-copying literals is the point). Shim rules learned the hard way: system includes live ONLY in shim.c (absorbing <pty.h>/<spawn.h> into the module collides with CDispatch's fd_set — the CNIOLinux pattern), and the dependency edge is UNCONDITIONAL (a `.when(platforms:)` TARGET dependency trips Xcode's `PQRCACPdynamic-product` "Multiple commands produce" planning bug, seen in Huginn); the shim compiles to an empty module on Apple, so PQRCACP still links zero external packages there. Linux slave handling differs deliberately: the parent closes its slave fd at once and the CHILD opens the slave BY PATH (`addopen` file action) — a fresh SETSID session leader acquiring its first tty via open(2) makes it the CONTROLLING terminal, which dup2'ing a parent-opened fd would not; no EIO race because glibc's posix_spawn (CLONE_VFORK) returns only after the file actions + exec ran. **The port caught a real kill-guarantee hole:** interactive bash re-enables job control even when invoked `+m` (zsh honors it), so a backgrounded job escapes the process-group SIGKILL — `terminate()` on Linux therefore adds a kernel-truth **/proc session sweep** (SIGKILL every pid whose session id == the child's, two bounded passes), which also closes the `setpgid`-escape hole for arbitrary executables. The no-orphan tests turned zombie-aware (`testProcessIsAlive` reads /proc state: a container's pid-1 test runner never reaps reparented children, so a KILLED child stays `kill(pid,0)==0` forever as a zombie). **EldrNodeCore.serve** drops the WS-L1 drain-stub and runs `runHarness` on Linux with `DefaultHarnessTransportFactory` — `.a2aRemote` delegation stays Apple-only (`A2AHarnessFactory`/A2AClient HTTP kept off the Linux path per AC139) and fails LOUDLY as `unsupportedKind` on a Linux node; the town A2A plane (RelayA2ATransport) is unaffected. **Proof, not inference:** the FULL 270-test PQRCACP suite is green in the local swift:6.2 container VM — PTY lifecycle + kill-the-orphan, `run_shell` via /bin/bash, path jail, StdioHarness perl/true spawns, and the RunnerE2E REAL-binary handshake (test locator now probes both bundle layouts) — and `eldr-node` builds on Linux; macOS stays byte-identical-green (270+8 PQRCACP, all eight packages, Huginn, EldrChat). CI's linux job now runs the whole PQRCACP suite (goose/hermes/cloud-CLI live handshakes self-skip where binaries are absent — skip, never vacuously pass). **Limits:** `.a2aRemote` on Linux refused (above); live `wss://` + TPM rung unchanged from AC139. | app-only |
| AC142 | **Pre-existing EldrChatTests link break — found (not caused) by the WS-L4 verification sweep, 2026-07-24.** At committed HEAD (2c088bf, before any WS-L4 change — proven by a clean-worktree bisect build), `xcodebuild test` for EldrChat failed to LINK the unit-test bundle: undefined `PQRCACP.JSONValue.*` / `ACPTerminal.interactiveTerminalTitlePrefix` / `ACPCloudDelegation.delegateTitlePrefix`. Cause: `EldrChatTests` never declared the `PQRCACP` product (only PQRCCore/PQRCNostr/PQRCAgent) and had been resolving those symbols through the HOST app via `-bundle_loader`; the app links package archives selectively (pull-on-need), so when earlier app-layer refactors shrank the app's own use of those members, the exported set silently lost exactly the symbols only the tests still referenced. The previous session's verification never reached the app suite (its PQRCACP suite was left red on the uncommitted AC140 test fix), so this landed unnoticed. Fix: declare `PQRCACP` on the test target (pbxproj product dependency + Frameworks phase, mirroring the existing PQRCCore pattern) so the tests link what they import instead of leaning on the app's incidental pull-set. Lesson re-learned twice this session and now house style: never pipe a gating build/test command into `tail` (and zsh has `$pipestatus`, not bash's `$PIPESTATUS`) — two green-looking exit-0s were `tail`'s, not xcodebuild's. | app-only |
