| AC144 | **Eldr↔Buzz interop SHIPPED (2026-07-24; WS-I/A/B/C from INTEROP-LANDSCAPE + BUZZ-NIP-INTEROP-PLAN).** Eldr now speaks Block/Buzz's agent-plane on the wire and hosts the local model as a first-class Buzz agent, with **zero Buzz-side changes** (INTEROP §8). **Crypto codecs (PQRCNostr, `[upstream-NIP]`), all proven byte-exact against Buzz's OWN published vectors** (`NIP-AE.md §Reference test vectors`, `buzz-sdk/nip_oa.rs`): `NIP44.swift` (NIP-44 v2 — ECDH raw-`shared_x` → HKDF-Extract salt `"nip44-v2"` → per-message HKDF-Expand → ChaCha20 + HMAC-SHA256(nonce‖ct); reproduces all four NIP-AE engram `content` fields + `K_c` exactly), `ChaCha20.swift` (RFC 8439 raw ChaCha20 — swift-crypto exposes only the `ChaChaPoly` AEAD, so the bare stream cipher is implemented from the IETF standard and pinned to RFC 8439 §2.4.2; **this is a published standard with published vectors, NOT a custom primitive, and it does NOT touch the PQRC core — `SealCipher` keeps its AEAD posture per SPEC §2**), `NIPOA.swift` (owner-attestation `auth` tags — verifies Buzz's spec signature, SHA-256 preimage match, conditions grammar), `BuzzEvents.swift` (kind 0/9/9000/10100 + NIP-AM 44200 + NIP-AO 24200 builders; tag layout mirrors `buzz-sdk/builders.rs`). The three NIP-AE re-derivation gotchas (raw unhashed `shared_x`; BIP-340 aux=0 folded not omitted — swift-secp256k1's 4-arg `strict:true` path already does this; `ensure_ascii=False` id serialization — `NostrEvent.canonicalSerialization` already preserves non-ASCII scalars) are pinned conformance assertions. `BuzzInteropCryptoTests` 12/12 green. **Gateway (WS-I5, `EldrBuzzGateway` + `eldr-buzz-agent`, `[app-only]`):** joins a Buzz relay (NIP-42 AUTH, standalone or NIP-OA owner-attested via the new `authenticate(extraTags:)` seam), publishes kind:0 profile, self-announces membership (kind:9000, rejection non-fatal), subscribes kind:9 by the NEW `NostrFilter.hTags`→`#h` channel filter, runs each mention against the LOCAL model (`OpenAICompatibleLLMClient` → Huginn :1337), posts a signed kind:9 reply threaded to the mention, and emits NIP-AM turn metrics (with the endpoint's REAL token counts via the additive `usageObserver`) + NIP-AO frames encrypted to the owner. **Proven end-to-end over a real loopback Nostr socket** (`NostrRelayServer`): `endToEnd_localModelRepliesAndEmitsMetric` (mock model + NIP-AM decrypt assertion) and **`endToEnd_realLocalModelReplies` (the real Qwen3.6-35B answering a Buzz `@mention`, 27 s, green)**. **Path B (`eldr-acp` as a Buzz managed agent):** `eldr-acp` answers the exact `buzz-acp` ACP handshake (proven smoke), but `buzz-acp` gates system-prompt delivery on ACP `protocolVersion≥2`/goose while `eldr-acp` reports v1, so the buzz-CLI reply convention must be injected via `ELDR_ACP_CONTEXT_FILE` — **wire-able, not run against a live Buzz relay (needs-owner)**. **Relay swap (source-grounded, `buzz-relay/handlers/ingest.rs`):** buzz-relay ACCEPTS `kind:1059` gift wraps (`MessagesWrite`) and `#p`-gates their reads exactly as Eldr's anchor relay does — so Eldr E2EE message *transport* is Buzz-relay-agnostic — but REJECTS Eldr's `kind:10420/10421/10422/1420/10050` identity/prekey events as "unknown event kind", so key *bootstrap* still needs an Eldr relay. Honest setting story: Eldr relay for identity + Buzz relay for transport, NOT a wholesale swap. **E2EE-termination cost (INTEROP §8.1, THREAT_MODEL §4b):** a Buzz channel is signed-not-E2EE, so the gateway is an edge bridge whose posted content is readable by the Buzz relay operator; it prints a startup disclosure and MUST be run only by the workspace owner. **Contributions (`docs/nips-contrib/`, PR-ready for `block/buzz/docs/nips`):** NIP-C1 Untrusted Data Admission (closes NIP-AE's named memory-poisoning hole; ref impl = `UntrustedDataEnvelope`/`TownWall`), NIP-C2 Sealed Attestation (NIP-OA provenance inside a NIP-59 seal, no public linkage), NIP-C3 Consent Windows (revocable human-signed time-boxed authorization under an untrusted relay; ref impl = `AgentEngine`). WS-I6: corrected `SettingsView.swift:501` "machine-readable header" → honest "display convention, not a wire format". **Verified:** PQRCNostr 126/126 + 12 Buzz-interop; EldrNode gateway suite green incl. both live E2Es. Full runbook: `docs/ELDR-BUZZ-INTEROP.md`. **[Correction, 2026-07-24 — recorded, not rewritten: the three contribution NIPs were renamed later the same day. NIP-C1 → **NIP-AD** (Untrusted Data Admission); NIP-C3 → **NIP-AC** (Agent Consent Windows); NIP-C2 "Sealed Attestation" was **withdrawn** and refiled as an amendment to NIP-OA, because the tag, preimage, grammar and signature were all NIP-OA's unchanged. Buzz uses two-letter codes and `NIP-CW` was already taken by Channel Window. See `docs/nips-contrib/README.md`.]** | upstream-NIP |
| AC145 | **The Phase-2 grant UI — phone visibility chips + the Huginn Town Grants panel (2026-07-24).** The two surfaces the workstream tracking had mis-filed as "device-gated": only the on-device VERIFICATION pass is — the UI itself is ordinary buildable work, now built. **Phone (invariant 9):** a live standing town grant pins a banner in the granted peer's 1:1 conversation for its whole day-scale lifetime (conversation ids ARE peer identity hexes for 1:1s; the matcher `AppModel.townGrantBanner` is pure/nonisolated and unit-tested incl. expiry and wrong-peer negatives), styled like the ai_window banner (opaque tint, A7 contrast). Managing lives INSIDE the existing C6 `ConversationAIContextSection` — extend-don't-fork, no new chrome per the UI-overload directive: per-peer rows (plane, day-scale time left, remaining day budgets) + Revoke, a mint menu (wall / wall+delegate · 7 days, bounded default budget 200 msg / 512 KiB / 4 tasks), and "Copy grant for the node" whose export JSON is EXACTLY a `town-grants.json` element (proven by decode+signature-verify in tests). Minting is phone-only — the human identity key lives there; `PersonaRuntime.startTownGrant/revokeTownGrant/townGrantStatuses` wrap the WS-G4 engine. Groups render no section (no single peer identity). Refresh is on-appear + after mutations, not a poll; remote-received grants surface on next appearance (stated simplification — no new runtime event case). **Huginn:** a Town Grants tab editing the node's `town-grants.json` + `town-peers.json` (ConfigPaths gains both): every entry re-verified (structure, signature, granter == the Bridge-pinned owner; unpaired = "signature OK — pair the phone to pin WHO signed") and labeled honestly — INVALID/FOREIGN/EXPIRED entries are SHOWN with the reason, never hidden, because the panel is the file's EDITOR while the node store is its fail-closed consumer; paste-import refuses anything that does not verify; Remove = the file store's revocation (AC143 contract); the roster editor's field names are pinned literally by a test so a rename on either side fails loudly instead of orphaning the roster. Huginn verifies + stages, never signs. **Proof:** EldrChat unit 104/104 (new lifecycle test: mint → banner visible → export decodes + human-signature verifies + names peer/planes → revoke → banner gone) + UI 16/16 TEST SUCCEEDED; Huginn TEST SUCCEEDED incl. 3 new panel tests. **Remaining for the owner:** the on-device eyeball pass (banner/section/tab look + paste flow), and the phone→Huginn grant hand-off is copy/paste in v1 (an automatic paired-channel push is future work, stated). | app-only |
| AC146 | **WS-I7 — "Connect my AI to a Buzz workspace" ships as a GUI (2026-07-24; both paths of `docs/ELDR-BUZZ-GUI-PLAN.md`, Phases 1–4).** AC144 built the engine and the proof; this is the product: a Huginn **Connections** tab where a user with zero terminal use puts the model this Mac is serving into a Buzz workspace as an agent. **Path A (Huginn-owned):** a 5-step `BuzzConnectWizardView` (workspace + live "Test connection" probe → model → identity/persona/channels → the E2EE-termination disclosure → connect) writing a `BuzzConnection` record, and `BuzzGatewayService` — an `MLXService`-shaped supervisor that spawns one `eldr-buzz-agent` child per connection (log file per connection, SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL, dies with the app, un-paused connections restored at launch). **Key handling (invariant 10):** each connection mints its OWN fresh agent key into the data-protection Keychain as `buzzagent.<id>` (never the owner's identity key, never shared between workspaces); the owner key is used ONCE in memory to compute the NIP-OA attestation and is never persisted — what persists is the auth TAG (a signature) and the owner's public key; the child receives the agent key only through its spawn environment, and `ELDR_BUZZ_OWNER_PRIVATE_KEY`/`ELDR_ACP_FAKE_LLM` are scrubbed out of the inherited environment (tested). **Fail-closed gates:** no acknowledged disclosure, no key, no channels, or a plaintext `ws://` relay off loopback (reusing `ConfigurationStore.validateRelayOverride`, so SPEC §0's rule has ONE implementation) each refuse to spawn with a stated reason. **Remove** publishes the agent-signed retirement — a kind:0 tombstone + a NIP-09 kind:5 deletion request for its own profile events (new `BuzzEvents.retirementProfile`/`profileDeletionRequest`) — and then destroys the key; the relay may refuse the events, and the honest outcome line says which part landed. **Rotate key** (Phase 4, the INTEROP §5.1 metadata concern) re-mints + re-attests; rotating WITHOUT the owner key deliberately DROPS the attestation rather than carrying a tag that names a dead key. **A design flaw in this plan's own §2 was found and fixed while building it:** the plan put "paste an invite/membership token" in Step 1, but a NIP-OA tag signs ONE specific agent pubkey — which does not exist until the connection is created — so an admin cannot attest it in advance and the field could only ever hold a tag that fails at the relay. The invited path now creates the connection first, shows the minted agent key with a Copy button, and takes the admin's tag afterwards in **Edit ▸ Attestation**, where `applyAttestation` VERIFIES it against this connection's own key (a tag for someone else's agent is refused at paste time, not discovered at connect time) and derives the owner pubkey from it. The same Edit sheet is the plan's "Edit persona" control (name/about/persona/channels/behavior/brain; relay + agent key deliberately immutable — changing either is a different attested identity, i.e. a new connection) and states when a save needs a restart to take effect. **Status is a contract, not log-scraping:** the new `BuzzGatewayStatus`/`BuzzGatewayCounters` (PQRCNostr — the one module both the gateway and Huginn link) defines the `ELDR-BUZZ-STATUS {json}` lines the gateway emits and the supervisor folds into the "● connected — 4 replies · 1.2k tokens" row; both directions are pinned by tests, and the loopback E2E now asserts the counters the GUI shows against the REAL emitter. **Egress firewall (Phase 4):** replies are run through `CredentialRedactor` before they cross into the signed-not-E2EE channel (`ELDR_BUZZ_REDACT`, default ON, per-connection toggle) — the same redactor the watch-along fan-out uses, at the exact hop where E2EE terminates. **Path B correctness fix — the AC144 snapshot generator was wrong and would have imported an agent that never answers:** Buzz's `definition.provider` is a provider **ID** projected into `GOOSE_PROVIDER` at spawn (`desktop/src-tauri/src/managed_agents/discovery.rs`), not a base URL, so `provider: "http://127.0.0.1:1337/v1"` = an unknown goose provider. `forLocalModel` now takes a `LocalProvider` (`lmstudio`/`openai`/`ollama`) and emits the provider id; because Buzz deliberately EXCLUDES env vars from snapshots (`agent_snapshot.rs` §Secret exclusion), the matching host variable is surfaced in the UI with a Copy button (`LMSTUDIO_HOST=http://127.0.0.1:1337`) instead of being silently omitted. **§8 build-order item 1 (verify B1) — answered from Buzz's + goose's own source, not a GUI click:** Buzz's agent dialog does expose a custom-provider field, and goose's `lmstudio` provider resolves `${LMSTUDIO_HOST}/v1/chat/completions`, so a Buzz-managed goose agent reaches Huginn's `:1337` with provider `lmstudio` + that one env var (already present in this machine's `~/.config/goose/config.yaml`). **`LogConsoleSource` gained an associated-value `buzzGateway(connectionID:name:)` case** (it is no longer a raw-value enum; `allCases` = the fixed picker sources, a per-connection log is routed in by the tab). **Also from the plan text:** Step 2 can **Serve it** a downloaded model inline (the MLX tab's own one-click `makeBrain`, so the model the agent will ask for is actually being served), and Step 3 takes an **avatar** — as a kind:0 `picture` **URL**, not an uploaded blob: the config crosses to the child as an environment variable, where a multi-MB data: URI would fail at spawn time rather than at edit time (and Eldr stores no one's images — invariant 4's text-only stance). **Record decoding is tolerant** (`init(from:)` with per-field defaults): the connections file decodes as a whole, so one field added by a later build would otherwise fail the decode and silently empty the user's list while orphaning live Keychain items; the two security-relevant defaults fall to the SAFE side — a record with no `redactOutbound` decodes with the egress firewall ON, and one with no `disclosureAcknowledged` decodes as NOT acknowledged. **Key storage reading, stated:** agent keys use `KeychainBox` (data-protection, `WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`, non-synced) rather than an explicit `MacSecureEnclaveKeyWrapper` wrap as the plan's §2 Step 5 suggested — matching how Huginn already stores the Bridge's own Nostr identity and the LLM/vendor keys. Invariant 10's SE-wrap requirement is scoped to the at-rest STORE master key (which is SE-wrapped, in `ConversationMemory`); a data-protection Keychain item is already SEP-protected on this hardware, and inventing a second wrap for it would be a divergence from house precedent, not an improvement. **Build:** a new Huginn build phase compiles + bundles `eldr-buzz-agent` exactly as the `eldr-acp` phase does, and `InstallerService` copies it to `~/.local/bin`. **Proof:** all 8 package suites green (incl. the loopback E2E, which now asserts the GUI's counters against the REAL status emitter), EldrChat 104/104, and **Huginn TEST SUCCEEDED with the 11 new tests**. Those tests earned their keep immediately by catching two real bugs on their first genuine run: (1) `persist()` encoded `createdAt` as ISO-8601 while `reload()` decoded with the default strategy — the file decodes as a WHOLE, so the throw dropped EVERY connection on the next launch while its Keychain key lived on (symmetric decoder + a non-throwing date fallback); (2) an injected executable path was trusted without an `isExecutableFile` check, so a missing binary surfaced as a raw spawn error instead of the actionable "isn't installed yet" pre-check. **Note on running them:** they are Keychain-backed, so a LOCKED screen makes them (and six pre-existing Keychain tests) fail with `errSecInteractionNotAllowed` (-25308) — that is `WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` working as specified, not a test defect. Run the Huginn suite unlocked. (b) No live hosted-Buzz-workspace run of the GUI flow (needs the owner's nsec + a real workspace) — the engine path it drives is the one AC144 proved live. (c) The "I was invited" path stores a pasted auth tag but there is no invite-token exchange. | app-only |

## buzz-mobile — WS-BM1/WS-BM2: the human workspace client + the invite on-ramp, 2026-07-24

Eldr's phone as a member of a Buzz workspace (`docs/ELDR-BUZZ-MOBILE-RELAY.md`). AC144 put an
Eldr-hosted *agent* in a Buzz channel; this is a *person*. Both workstreams are implemented and
green in `PQRCNostr` (`BuzzWorkspaceClientTests`, 34 tests). The app surface (WS-BM3) and the
encrypted plane (WS-BM4) are NOT built and are not claimed here. Nothing was run against a live
Buzz relay — the wire facts below are read from Buzz's Rust/Dart source, not exercised against
`communities.buzz.xyz`.

| # | Decision | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| AC147 | **A Buzz workspace is joined with NIP-98 over their invite REST route, not with the NIP-OA attestation ceremony `ELDR-BUZZ-PAIRING.md` designed.** That doc's Options A/B authorize an *agent/gateway* key and require `BUZZ_ALLOW_NIP_OA_AUTH` (default false) or an operator inserting an allowlist row — both need someone on Buzz's side to act. A *human* has a strictly easier door: `POST /api/invites/claim`, NIP-98-signed by the joining pubkey and **deliberately exempt from the relay-membership gate** (`buzz-relay/src/api/invites.rs:289` — "the whole point is that the caller is not a member yet"), which is exactly what Buzz's own Flutter client uses. So `NIP98.swift` + `BuzzInvite.swift` ship the link grammar (`https://<relay>/invite/<code>` and the `buzz://join` handoff), the join-policy/receipt dance, and the claim, with the relay's coarse error strings (`invite_expired` / `invite_invalid` / `join_policy_required`) mapped to typed errors. The claim is idempotent server-side, so `already_member` is a success, not a failure. **Which key claims is left to the caller and the app must mint a fresh per-workspace one** (privacy-maximising per CLAUDE.md's tie-breaker, and what Buzz's own client does): reusing the Eldr identity key would publish a permanent, public correlation between Eldr identity and Buzz activity — the same irreversible cost `ELDR-BUZZ-PAIRING.md §3` records for Option A. | `[app-only]` |
| AC148 | **PQRC's own kinds are NOT renumbered to fit Buzz, and never should be.** `required_scope_for_kind` (`buzz-relay/src/handlers/ingest.rs:303`) is a **closed allowlist** whose fall-through is `Err("restricted: unknown event kind")`. 10420/10421/10422/10050 are rejected because they are absent from *someone else's registry*, not because they collide with anything — renumbering would break our published NIP and fix nothing, and picking a number Buzz *does* accept would be worse (our bytes would be interpreted as their kind). The two supported answers are therefore (a) keep an Eldr relay for identity alongside a Buzz relay for transport, or (b) carry the bundle inside a kind Buzz already accepts. A conformance test (`emittedKindsAreInBuzzRegistry`) pins both halves: every kind we emit is in their registry, and the PQRC kinds are disjoint from it. | `[upstream-NIP]` |
| AC149 | **kind:0 is treated as a shared, merge-on-write record, never rebuilt from scratch.** Buzz validates only that kind:0 content is valid JSON (`ingest.rs:2234`), syncs just `display_name`/`name`/`picture`/`image`/`about`/`nip05` (`side_effects.rs:1113`), and stores the raw content verbatim — which is what makes a profile a viable carrier for an Eldr prekey bundle (WS-BM4) with zero Buzz changes. But kind:0 is **absolute state** ("fields present are set; fields absent are cleared"), so any client that republishes a fresh object silently destroys every field it does not know about — including Buzz's own, and including ours. `BuzzProfile` therefore preserves `rawContent` and `publishProfile` requires the existing profile to merge into. This is a correctness requirement, not tidiness: the failure it prevents is a user editing their display name in Buzz Desktop and silently dropping their encrypted-DM bundle. | `[app-only]` |
| AC150 | **`RelayTransport` grows an optional `subscribeFrames` surfacing the NIP-01 EOSE boundary.** `subscribe(_:)` deliberately hides stored-vs-live because PQRC's own traffic never needs it. A workspace client does: "the roster" and "one screen of history" are bounded questions, and without the boundary the only way to answer them is a guessed timeout. Added as a **defaulted** protocol method (forwarding `subscribe`, never synthesising a boundary) so every existing conformer is untouched; `NostrWebSocketTransport` implements it off the real EOSE frame and `LocalRelayConnection` off the pre-existing atomic `handleSubscribeSplit`. `BuzzWorkspaceClient.collectStored` still carries a deadline as the backstop and returns partial results rather than hanging — it never claims a truncated result is complete. `NostrFilter` gains `#d` (NIP-29 addressable state) and `limit` alongside. | `[app-only]` |
| AC151 | **Over-long workspace content throws; the client will not split or truncate a message on the user's behalf.** Buzz caps event content and Eldr's `RelayFraming` chunking is meaningless there — a Buzz relay has no PQRC reassembly, so chunks would land as garbage fragments in a channel other people read. `BuzzWorkspaceError.contentTooLong(bytes:limit:)` surfaces the limit and lets the product decide visibly. Same reasoning as invariant 4's refusal to inline >64 KB: silent adaptation to someone else's limit is how content gets mangled. | `[app-only]` |
| AC152 | **A Buzz workspace is a separate surface in the app, never folded into the conversation list.** An Eldr conversation is PQ-ratcheted end-to-end; a Buzz channel is signed-but-plaintext and its operator reads every word. Merging them into one list of "chats" would reduce the single most important security difference in the product to a styling detail — so `BuzzWorkspaceModel` is a sibling of `AppModel` (not a mode inside it), workspaces get their own sidebar section below conversations, and `BuzzChannelView` deliberately does **not** reuse `MessageBubble`. The channel carries a standing "Readable by `<host>`" banner (the `ai_window` posture: a standing capability must stay visible for its whole lifetime, not be disclosed once), and the first send anywhere requires acknowledging the boundary — having joined a workspace is not consent to post plaintext into it. Routing rides `MainView`'s existing `String?` selection as a `buzz:<workspace>/<channel>` prefix rather than widening the type; a workspace route **never** falls through to `ConversationView`, since a `buzz:` id is not a conversation id and rendering it as one would look like a broken chat. | `[app-only]` |
| AC153 | **A failed workspace join destroys the key it minted.** `BuzzWorkspaceRegistry.join` mints the per-workspace key *before* claiming (the claiming pubkey is what becomes a member, so it must exist first), which means an invite that turns out to be expired/invalid would otherwise leave a usable credential in the Keychain for a workspace the user never joined. The claim is wrapped so any failure deletes `buzzws.<id>` before rethrowing, and `forget` likewise deletes the key rather than only dropping the record — a leftover key is a live workspace credential on a device the user believes they have left. Both are pinned by tests. Note the local-only scope: Buzz's own membership row is the relay's to remove, and a Nostr relay keeps what was already published either way. | `[app-only]` |
| AC154 | **Documentation lifecycle formalised; `docs/` reorganised (2026-07-27).** `docs/` had grown to 39 flat files / 19,414 lines with no index, and the answer to "what is left to build?" was spread across 13 documents, this ledger, and two gitignored files in `private/`. **The cause was one specific gap: plans had no exit.** A plan was written, partly built, marked "IMPLEMENTED" at the top with the original preserved below, and left in `docs/` — becoming three documents at once (plan, status board, archive), each wanting a different shape. Because finished plans never left, facts were *copied into the next plan instead of referenced*: the E2EE-termination rule existed five times, and one copy still said `NIP-C1/C2/C3` twenty-four hours after the rename. **The repo already had the rule** (`docs/deprecated/README.md`); it last fired 2026-07-17 and everything after piled up. **Shape now:** 11 files at `docs/` root (the four canon docs + THREAT_MODEL + the CC0 extensions + the ledger + two new navigational files), then `guide/` (19, how-to), `pitch/` (3, positioning), `plan/` (designs for unbuilt work only), `done/<YYYY-MM-DD>/` (19, retired, read-only, renamed from `deprecated/` and dated by retirement), `nips-contrib/` (7, outbound). **Two new files carry the load:** `docs/README.md` (the index) and **`docs/BACKLOG.md` — the single list of unbuilt work**, mined from every doc, all 153 prior AC entries, and `private/`; its top finding is that *a second machine blocks six separate workstreams*. **The rule is now in CLAUDE.md** (§ Documentation lifecycle): every doc is law / guide / ledger / plan; the first three are edited in place; a plan expires into `done/` the moment its work lands here — never edited into a status report and left. Step 4 of the exit is "re-point every inbound reference **including Swift source comments**", added because `ACPRouterplan.md` was archived on 2026-07-17 and seven files under `Packages/PQRCACP/` were left pointing at the dead path for ten days (repointed now, with 9 more across `PQRCNostr`/`App`/`Apps`). Step 5 is "sweep BACKLOG.md", without which the single list stops being the answer. **14 stale facts were found and fixed while re-filing** — each verified against code or the artefact, not inferred. The three that would have cost real time: (a) `OSS-RELEASE-RUNBOOK` Step 4 claimed `eldr-acp/Package.swift` still had a local-path dependency and gave a `perl` one-liner to rewrite it — the manifest already declares the URL form, so the substitution matched nothing and *reported success*; the repo is also named `swift-a2a`, not `a2a-swift` (8 sites), so `gh repo create` would have made a wrongly-named repo whose dependency URL 404s. (b) `OSS-EXTRACTION-AUDIT` gave two different numbers for the same measurement (§5: 489/472, §7: 477/460) — counted from the real repos, 489 is correct. (c) `SETUP-GUIDE` said "**All three** must be green" under a list of five packages when there are eight. Also: `THREAT_MODEL` linked a file deleted the same day; a **CC0 doc intended for upstream publication** cited gitignored `private/GOOSEWORLD.md`; `DEMO.md` presented the permanently-rejected Blossom path as a demo feature (the simulators *are* still constructed — `LocalUniverse.swift:34-35` — so the fact was kept and labelled vestigial rather than deleted); and `plan/ELDR-BUZZ-MOBILE-RELAY.md` declared workstream prefix `WS-M`, already owned by the Huginn MLX overhaul (AC113–AC121). **Nothing was deleted and no prose was reworded** — of 58 documents, 26 are byte-identical in place, 12 byte-identical but moved, 21 edited (the fact fixes, archive headers, and link re-pointing), 0 lost. Corrections to AC107 and AC144 were **appended**, not rewritten. `private/GOOSEWORLD.md` is a 139-of-190-line fork of the public copy and is recommended for deletion, not deleted here. **Deliberately not fixed:** the 39 links in `nips-contrib/` that resolve inside `block/buzz/docs/nips/` — they are supposed to be broken here, and the folder README now says so. **Still open (in BACKLOG §8):** `guide/SETUP-GUIDE.md` is 540 lines and is really two documents (§9 is a ~240-line `eldr-acp` operator manual that two other docs defer to as canonical), and the E2EE-termination disclosure still exists twice in live docs. | app-only |
| AC155 | **WS-D: the World dashboard — one answer to "who am I connected to?", in both apps (2026-07-28).** Connection state was spread across three surfaces that could not see each other and one process no UI could read: towns in `town-peers.json`/`town-grants.json` (with the only liveness signal, `TownWallHost.lastSeen`, held *in memory by the running node*), Buzz in `BuzzConnectionStore`/`BuzzWorkspaceRegistry`, tethers in `ConfiguredAI`. The concrete failure this produced, hit live while building: Huginn's Town Grants panel truthfully rendered `None` while a node was serving a granted town, because the panel reads `~/.config/eldr-acp/town-grants.json` and the node reads `$ELDR_TOWN_GRANTS_FILE` or `<workdir>/.eldr/town-grants.json`. **Node (WS-D1n):** new `TownStatusSnapshot` + `TownWallHost.statusSnapshot()`, served as a `world/status` JSON-RPC method **intercepted in `GooseworldSocketHost` ahead of `GooseworldMCPServer`** — deliberately NOT an MCP tool, so no goose flock can enumerate towns in machine-readable form and the four injection-hardened, CC0-donatable tool descriptions are byte-unchanged (asserted: absent from `tools/list`, refuses when invoked as a tool). It reuses the socket's existing token gate and loopback bind rather than opening a second door, is read-only, and carries **no message content and no peer identity hex** (views join on `townID`; pinned by an allowlist canary with a real wall post present, so the absence is proven rather than vacuous). `statusSnapshot()` reuses the SAME `grantCovers` predicate as `towns()`, so the human and agent views cannot disagree about who is authorized. **Huginn (WS-D2h):** `WorldStatusClient` (mirror wire type — Huginn deliberately does not link EldrNode; drift pinned by test) + a `World` tab that **consolidates** the former `Connections` and `Town Grants` tabs, both of which survive intact as detail panes so every import/edit/revoke path is unchanged. **Phone (WS-D1a):** a `World` sidebar peer over `myTownGrants` + `BuzzWorkspaceRegistry` + `tetheredAIList()`, no new protocol. **The load-bearing rule is that `authorized` and `connected` are different facts and never collapse** — a grant is permission, not a connection. Authorization is checked BEFORE recency, so a town revoked one second ago reads `not authorized` despite a fresh `lastSeen` (otherwise the kill switch would look like it had failed); and because the phone cannot observe liveness, `PhoneGrantStatus` has **no `connected` case at all** — unrepresentable, not merely unused, with an exhaustive-switch tripwire. **WS-D3 map:** a radial `Canvas` map toggled beside the list in both apps (the list remains the accessible surface; the map carries a summary label). Animation is scoped to what each device can verify: the Mac moves a pulse along an edge to encode the *state* "traffic seen inside `liveWindowSeconds`" (not one dot per message — stated in the legend), while the phone draws **every** edge dashed and still and instead animates the grant clock it genuinely owns, breathing only inside the final day. Lifetime rings normalize against `PQRCConstants.maxStandingGrantDuration`, the protocol's own cap, rather than an invented denominator. **Deviations from the approved plan, both owner-chosen:** the node-served web dashboard is deferred (native-first; Linux towns get no dashboard yet — see BACKLOG), and Buzz is INCLUDED, the plan's "Buzz = document-only" decision having predated the Buzz code that shipped in AC147–153. **Rider:** `AgentEngine.StandingGrantStatus` gained a public memberwise init — a public read-only DTO whose synthesized init was `internal` and therefore unconstructible by app-layer tests. **Proof:** 8 packages green (Core 104 / Nostr 179 / Agent 121 / ACP 275+8 / MCP 110 / Node 80 / ctl 22 / A2A 89), Huginn + EldrChat build, 16 Huginn and 12 phone tests, and `world/status` driven over the REAL socket against a live node — returning `town-b` with both planes granted and `grantExpiry` 1785813460, matching both `world_towns` and the `active_until` of the phone-minted grant that produced it. **Unverified:** the two-machine ritual (unchanged), and the node-served web dashboard (not built). | app-only |
